Backgrounds
Backgrounds are presented in alphabetical order.

### Acolyte of the Gardner

You served the god of nature.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind

**Stamina:** 5

**Trait:** Enchantment: Material Transfer
**+1 Skills:** Handle Animal, Nature Lore, Navigate,
Religious Lore, Swim, Benefaction, Elemental
**Equipment:** Holy symbol, torch, spellbooks: fire
hands, minor healing, spark

### Acolyte of the Healer
You served the god of healing.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind

**Stamina:** 7

**Trait:** Benefaction: Enhanced Healing
**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Lift, Religious Lore,
Benefaction, Bashing
**Equipment:** Holy symbol, lantern, mace, oil
flask, spellbooks: minor blessing, minor healing

### Acolyte of the Smith

You served the god of creativity.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind

**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Enchantment: Hands for Tools
**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Blacksmithing, Enchanting,
Religious Lore, Benefaction

**Equipment:** Holy symbol, magnifying glass,
torch, spellbooks: minor healing, summon object

### Acolyte of the Three
You served the god of death, greeed, and knowledge.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind or Strength
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Necromancy: Soul Absorption
**+1 Skills:** Magic Lore, Monster Lore, Religious
Lore, Necromancy, Chopping
**Equipment:** Axe, torch, spellbooks: bone capture,
minor curse, monster sense

### Acolyte of the Warrior
You served the god of war.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind or Strength

**Stamina:** 9

**Trait:** Benefaction: First Responder

**+1 Skills:** Religious Lore, Benefaction, Chopping

**Equipment:** Axe, holy symbol, torch, spellbooks:
minor healing, minor ward

### Alchemist
You crafted and sold acids, potions, and bombs.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Alchemy: Midnight Oil
**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Enchanting, Magic Lore,

Monster Lore, Sabotage, Search, Bow

**Equipment:** Acid vial, alchemist’s tools, bucket,
healing potion, lantern, oil flask

### Apprentice Mage
You studied several magic disciplines under a more
experienced tutor.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind

**Stamina:** 5

**Trait:** Alteration: Alteration Stone

**+1 Skills:** Enchanting, Magic Lore, Monster
Lore, Alteration, Conjuration, Elemental, Illusion
**Equipment:** Spellbooks: acid spit, jaunt, light, take
shape, thunder

### Archer

You served as a soldier or member of the watch who
was specially trained with a bow.
**+1 Characteristic:** Agility

**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Archery: Point Blank
**+1 Skills:** Climb, Hide, Sneak, Bow, Unarmed
**Equipment:** Quiver of 20 arrows, shortbow,
spyglass, torch, whistle

### Assassin
You were a killer who either worked paid contracts or
served a higher purpose in a shady organization.

**+1 Characteristic:** Agility
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Thievery: Sieze the Advantage
**+1 Skills:** Climb, Gymnastics, Hide, Pick Lock,
Sneak, Bow, Slashing
**Equipment:** Extra knife, poison vial, quiver of
20 arrows, shortbow, torch

### Beggar
You relied on the kindness of strangers to scrape
together enough coins to live.

**+1 Characteristic:** Any
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Scavenging: More for Less
**+1 Skills:** Endurance, Hide, Lift, Search, Sneak
**Equipment:** Lock, pot, string, tent, torch

### Blacksmith
You forged and sold items made from metal.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 7

**Trait:** Smithing: Double Duty

**+1 Skills:** Blacksmithing, Endurance, Lift,
Sabotage, Bashing
**Equipment:** Bell, chain, maul, smith’s tools,
torch

### Bodyguard
You protected a person of importance.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 9

**Trait:** Armor: Interposing Arm

**+1 Skills:** Endurance, Hide, Slashing

**Equipment:** Ball bearings, light armor, shield,
sword, torch

### Cartographer
You drew maps of villages, ruins, or wilderness areas
so others could find their way.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Travel: Orienteering
**+1 Skills:** Historical Lore, Monster Lore, Nature
Lore, Navigate, Search
**Equipment:** Compass, journal, lantern, lore
book (historical), oil flask, quill and inkpot

### Conjurer
You studied conjuration magic on your own or with
a teacher.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Conjuration: Jumper
**+1 Skills:** Handle Animal, Magic Lore, Monster
Lore, Navigate, Conjuration
**Equipment:** Chain, torch, spellbooks: jaunt,
summon object, teleport object

### Cook
You worked as a cook at a tavern or organization.

**+1 Characteristic:** Any

**Stamina:** 7

**Trait:** Camping: Hearty Meals
**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Endruance, Lift, Nature
Lore, Slashing
**Equipment:** Cook’s utensils, hearty ration (2),
pot, torch

### Duelist
You dedicated your youth to fencing in noble courts.

**+1 Characteristic:** Agility

**Stamina:** 9

**Trait:** Slashing: Finesse the Blade

**+1 Skills:** Gymnastics, Historical Lore, Slashing
**Equipment:** Light armor, lore book (historical),
mirror, sword, torch

### Entertainer
You were an acrobat, dancer, and/or musician.

**+1 Characteristic:** Agility
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Camping: Song of Rest
**+1 Skills:** Climb, Endurance, Gymnastics, Handle
Animal, Historical Lore, Jump, Sleight of Hand
**Equipment:** 10-foot pole, chalk, mirror, musical
instrument (lute), torch

### Executioner
You carried out capital punishment.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 9

**Trait:** Chopping: Chop ‘Em Down
**+1 Skills:** Lift, Chopping, Unarmed

**Equipment:** Chain, greataxe, ladder, lock, torch

### Farmer

You ploughed the fields and raise animals.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Pets: Buddy
**+1 Skills:** Blacksmithing, Endurance, Handle
Animal, Lift, Nature Lore
**Equipment:** 10-foot pole, animal feed (6), goat
(pet), pot, torch

### Gladiator

You fought deadly battles for entertainment of others.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 9
**Trait:** Stabbing: Bury the Point

**+1 Skills:** Jump, Lift, Stabbing
**Equipment:** Light armor, mirror, pike, rage
potion, torch

### Hunter
You tracked animals in the wild and foraged for food.

**+1 Characteristic:** Agility
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Travel: Foraging Expert
**+1 Skills:** Handle Animal, Hide, Nature Lore,
Navigate, Sneak, Swim, Bow
**Equipment:** Animal feed (6), dog (pet), quiver of
20 arrows, shortbow torch

### Hydromancer
You learned how to cast elemental spells that create
and harness water.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind

**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Elemental: Water Shield

**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Enchanting, Magic Lore,
Swim, Elemental
**Equipment:** Jounral, quill and inkpot, torch,
spellbooks: create water, stream

### Illusionist
You studied illusion magic either on your own or with
a teacher.
**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Illusion: Lasting Illusion

**+1 Skills:** Hide, Magic Lore, Monster Lore, Pick
Lock, Search, Sneak, Illusion

**Equipment:** Card deck, whistle, spellbooks:
cacophony, light, minor phantasm

### Keraunomancer
You learned how to cast elemental spells that create
and harness lightning and thunder.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Elemental: Hurl the Storm
**+1 Skills:** Blacksmith, Enchanting, Magic Lore,
Elemental, Bashing
**Equipment:** Mace, mirror, torch, spellbooks:
spark, thunder

### Knight
You were a knight in the service of a noble or a hedge
knight in service to no one other than yourself.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 9
**Trait:** Slashing: Swordplay
**+1 Skills:** Handle Animal, Historical Lore,
Slashing
**Equipment:** Animal feed (6), greatsword, horse
(pet), lantern, light armor, oil flask

### Merchant
You were a merchant who traveled from village to
village, tading your wares.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind

**Stamina:** 7

**Trait:** Camping: Plotting
**+1 Skills:** Historical Lore, Lift, Navigate, Search,
Sleight of Hand
**Equipment:** Journal, lantern, merchant’s scales,
oil flask, quill and ink pot, 50 gold coins

### Miner
You worked in mines, digging and hauling ore.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 7
**Trait:** Scavenging: Lasting Light
**+1 Skills:** Climb, Endurance, Lift, Navigate,
Stabbing
**Equipment:** Chalk, grappling hook, lantern, oil
flask, pickaxe, shovel

### Noble
You grew up in a family that had wealth and power.

**+1 Characteristic:** Agility or Mind
**Stamina:** 7

**Trait:** Reputation: Call Daddy

**+1 Skills:** Handle Animal, Historical Lore,
Religious Lore, Sneak, Bow
**Equipment:** Animal feed (6), horse (pet),
lantern, lore book (historical), oil flask, 50 gold
coins

### Pugilist
You made a living battling it out with bare knuckles.

**+1 Characteristic:** Agility or Strength
**Stamina:** 9
**Trait:** Unarmed: Pack a Punch
**+1 Skills:** Climb, Jump, Unarmed
**Equipment:** Chalk, light armor, lore book
(monsters), rage potion, torch

### Pyromancer
You learned how to cast elemental spells that create
and harness fire.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 5

**Trait:** Elemental: Make It Burn!

**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Enchanting, Magic Lore,
Swim, Elemental
**Equipment:** magnifying glass, string, torch,
spellbooks: fire hands, fire lance

### Sage
You studied all subjects with a tutor or by burying
your nose in many books (likely both).

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Knowledge: Cram
**+1 Skills:** Historical Lore, Magic Lore, Monster
Lore, Nature Lore, Navigate, Religious Lore,
Sabotage
**Equipment:** Journal, lore book (monsters), lore
book (nature), quill and ink pot, torch

### Soldier
You served as a soldier in an army dedicated to
protecting others from the threats of the wilderness.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength

**Stamina:** 9

**Trait:** Armor: Stalwart
**+1 Skills:** Endurance, Bow, Stabbing
**Equipment:** Light armor, quiver of 20 arrows,
shortbow, spear, torch

### Thief
You burgled places for money, either on your own or
as part of a guild.
**+1 Characteristic:** Agility

**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Thievery: Stealthy
**+1 Skills:** Gymnastics, Hide, Pick Lock, Sabotage,
Search, Sleight of Hand, Sneak

**Equipment:** Crowbar, grappling hook, knife
(extra), lockpick set, torch

### Tinkerer

You created magic and mechanical inventions.

**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Knowledge: Improvised Equipment
**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Blacksmithing, Enchanting,
Pick Lock, Sabotage, Search, Chopping

**Equipment:** Alchemist’s tools, blacksmith’s
tools, handaxe, lockpick set, torch

### Transmuter

You studied alteration magic either on your own or
with a teacher.
**+1 Characteristic:** Mind
**Stamina:** 5
**Trait:** Alteration: Lasting Alteration
**+1 Skills:** Alchemy, Blacksmithing, Enchanting,
Magic Lore, Pick Lock, Sabotage, Alteration
**Equipment:** Chalk, torch, spellbooks: jaunt,
animal form, repair take, shape
### Village Watch

You were a member of an organized law enforcement
body that kept a settlement safe.

**+1 Characteristic:** Strength
**Stamina:** 9
**Trait:** Polearm: Begone
**+1 Skills:** Jump, Search, Stabbing
**Equipment:** Chain, lantern, light armor, oil
flask, pike, whistle

## Advancement
Whenever you return to your village (see
Village) and finish a rest after looting a dungeon,
you gain experience points (XP) equal to the
combined value of all the treasure your group
brought back in gold coins divided by the
number of players (not characters) in the party.
Unique items don’t have a cost. Their XP value is
10,000, or 500 if it can become useless after use.

You can spend XP on new traits (see Traits). You
should also keep track of the total XP your PC
gains over the course of their adventuring career
(called total XP or TXP).

The Skill and Stamina Advancement table shows
the TXP required for your PC to increase their
skill bonuses or Stamina. When you get a bonus,
choose one of the following options:
- Increases two skill’s bonuses (inlcuding skills in
which you don’t currently have a bonus) by 1
(to a maximum of +2).
- Increase your Stamina maximum by 4.

- Increases one skill’s bonus (inlcuding skills in
which you don’t currently have a bonus) by
1 (to a maximum of +2), and increase your
Stamina maximum by 2.

Skill and Stamina Advancement
| TXP | Bonus |
| --- | --- |
| 100 | 1st |
| 500 | 2nd |
| 1,250 | 3rd |
| 2,250 | 4th |
| 3,500 | 5th |
| 5,000 | 6th |
| Every 5,000 after | 7th and up |

The Characteristics Advancement table shows
the TXP required for your PC to increase one of
their characteristics by 1, to a maximum of 3. If
all of your characteristics are 3 when you get this
bonus, you instead gain 4 Stamina.
Characteristics Advancement
| TXP | Bonus |
| --- | --- |
| 5,000 | 1st |
| 15,000 | 2nd |
| Every 15,00 after | 3rd and up |

New PC After Death
If your PC dies and you make a new PC to take
their place in the same campaign, you can roll on
the Backgrounds table an additional number of
times equal to the dead PC’s number of bonuses
on the Skill and Stamina Advancement table and
choose any background for the new PC from the
results you get.

Starting With More
If all of the other crows in the party have 5,000
or more TXP when you you create a new PC,
your character lags significantly behind the
others in terms of power and survivability. This
imbalance is a fun challenge for some players to
offset with caution, equipment, and clever tactics.
The disparity doesn’t last long. The new PC
gains XP fast with experienced crows. For other
groups, the new PC is a burden that leaves some
folks feeling annoyed or ignored. To offset this
uneveness, the Ref can decided that a new PC
starts with XP equal to the lowest TXP of a crow
already in the party.
It’s unlikely that a new crow will need better
equipment than what they start with, since their

dead counterpart likely left gear behind that
they’ll get, but the Ref can give the PC gold pieces
equal to half their starting XP to be used to buy
better equipment. This money must be spent on

equipment and not upgrading village institutions.

## Traits

Traits are character options that enhance your
PC’s ability to create and use equipment. Your
background grants you a trait. Traits are themed
and grouped in trees.

Buying Traits

Whenever you finish a rest, you can spend XP to
buy one or more traits. You can only purchase a
starting trait on a trait tree or a trait connected
by a line to another trait you already have on
the same tree. Starting traits appear at the top
of trait trees and cost 500 XP each. You can only
purchase a trait once.

Minimum Modifier
Whenever a trait increases or decreases a
number equal to one of your characteristics, the
minimum number of that increase or decrease is
1, even if your characteristic is lower.

Trait Trees

Trait trees are presented in alphabetical order.
| Tree | Specialization |
| --- | --- |
| Alchemy | Creating alchemy items |
| Alteration | Alteration spells |
| Archery | Bow weapons |
| Armor | Armor and shields |
| Bashing | Bashing weapons |
| Benefaction | Benefaction spells |
| Blacksmithing | Creating armor and weapons |
| Camping | Rest activies |
| Chopping | Chopping weapons |
| Conjuration | Conjuration spells |
| Elemental | Elemental spells |
| Enchantment | Creating magic items |
| Illusion | Illusion spells |
| Knowledge | General lore |
| Leverage | Getting the most out of gear |
| Necromancy | Necromancy spells |
| Pets | Directing and raising animals |
| Reputation | Villages |
| Slashing | Slashing weapons |
| Stabbing | Stabbing weapons |
| Thievery | Breaking and entering |
| Travel | Overland travel |
| Unarmed | Unarmed attacks |

### Alchemy
For creating and using acid, poisons, potions, and bombs

#### Midnight Oil
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you work on crafting
an alchemy item as part of the
Craft Equipment rest activity,
you can make two crafting rolls
for the item or make a second
crafting roll for a different
alchemy item.

#### Alchemy Epiphany
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you make a crafting roll
for an alchemy item, you get
a crit when the dice without
modifiers equal 18, 19, or 20.

#### Eschew Ingredients
**XP Cost: 1,500**
You can ignore the material
requirements for crafting an
alchemy item if the item’s
crafting goal is 100 or lower.

#### Two for One
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you finishing crafting an
alchemy item, you craft two of
the item instead of one.

#### It Burns
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
Acids and poisons you craft
deal additional damage equal to
twice your Mind. Bombs you
craft deal additional damage
equal to your Mind.

#### Big Boom
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Bombs you craft have the size
of the cube they effect increased
by 1.

#### You In Particular
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you use a bomb, choose
one creature who must make
the RR against its effects. That
creature takes -2 penalty to the
RR against the bomb.

#### Don’t Waste a Drop
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You can use an acid or a poison
twice before it is consumed.

#### Potent Potion
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
Healing potions you craft
restore additional Stamina
equal to your Mind or
remove 1 additional wound
if the drinker is already at full
Stamina.

#### Lasting Brew
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Potions and poisons you craft
that have a duration listed in
UD gain 1 additional UD.

#### Potion to Gas
**XP Cost: 1,500**
As an action, you can turn a
potion you carry into a gas.
The potion is immediately
consumed and two creatures of
your choice within 1 square of
you, including you, are affected
as if they drank the potion.

#### Alchemy Belt
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You gain a third belt slot
that can only be used to hold
alchemy items.

### Alteration
For maximal use of alteration spells

#### Lasting Alteration
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast an alteration
spell with a duration listed in
UD, add an addtioinal 1 UD to
the duration.

#### Alteration Mastery
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Non-doom tier 1 results of
rank 0 and 1 alteration spells
you cast don’t add to the chaos
count. Your alteration spells of
rank 2 and higher are treated
as 2 ranks lower when they
trigger a backlash.

#### Improved Animal
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast an alteration
spell that allows you to turn
into an animal, you can turn
into an animal with power
equal to what the spell says plus
your Mind.

#### Split Alteration
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast an alteration
spell that has a target of 1
creature, object, ally, enemy,
or target, you can target one
additional qualifying target.

#### Grow Scales
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast an alteration
spell, you can harness the magic
to grow temporary scales on
your body and gain AD equal to
your Mind. The AD granted by
this trait disappears at the end
of a rest.

#### Hooves
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you cast an altearation
spell, you can harness the
magic to change your feet
into powerful hooves until
the end of the DT. While you
have these hooves, your speed
increase by your Mind.

#### Horns
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast an altearation
spell, you can harness the magic
to grow horns from your head
that last until the end of the
DT. You can make an unarmed
attack with the horns as a
maneuver.

#### Wings
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast an altearation
spell, you can harness the magic
to grow wings that last until the
end of the DT. While you have
these wings, you gain a flying
speed equal to your speed.

#### Alteration Stone
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
As a rest activity, you can create
an alteration stone (which has
an equipment card). This stone
disappears if you create a new
one.

#### Morphing Stone
**XP Cost: 1,000**
A creautre who holds the stone
can use a maneuver to change it
into the shape of a mace, maul,
flail, or back to its original
form. The stone gains the
stats of its weapon form while
retaining its own stats.

#### Movement Stone
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Add the following benefits to
those your stone can confer:
- You can breathe underwater
and gain a swimming speed
equal to your speed.
- You gain a climbing speed
equal to your speed.

#### Spell Stone
**XP Cost: 2,000**
Choose up to three alteration
spellbooks in your possession.
The stone absorbs and destorys
those books. A creature
wielding the stone has those
spellbooks equipped. The stone
can only hold three spells.

### Archery
For mastery of bows

#### Point Blank
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you make a ranged
attack with a bow, you don’t
suffer the -1 penalty for having
an enemy within 1 square of
you.

**XP Cost: 1,000**
You can use a maneuver to aim
while you have a bow weapon

with that weapon before the
end of this turn, the attack
gains a +1 bonus.

#### Walls Can’t Save You
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Attacks you make with bows
don’t suffer a penalty for the
target having cover.

#### Split Shot
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you make an attack with
a bow, you can use twice as
much ammunition and select
two targets within range for
the attack.

#### Range Finder
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
While you wield a bow
weapon, it’s range increases by
2 squares. This bonus stacks
with Greater Range.

#### Greater Range
**XP Cost: 1,000**
While you wield a bow
weapon, it’s range increases by
2 squares. This bonus stacks
with Range Finder.

#### Shot in the Dark
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Attacks you make with bows
don’t suffer a penalty for the
target being in dim light or
similar concealment, and they
only suffer a -2 penalty against
target in darkness or similar
concealment.

#### Careful Shot
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you get a non-doom tier
1 result on an attack made with
a bow, you don’t hit an ally
within 1 square of the target.

#### Prone Position
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
If you are prone, you gain a +1
bonus on attacks made with
crossbows.

#### Scrap Arrows
**XP Cost: 1,000**
As a rest activity, you can turn
sticks and scraps of nearby
wood into arrows and gain 2d6
arrows when you finish the
rest.

#### Double Tap
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you make an attack with
a bow, you can use two pieces
of ammunition per target. If the
attack hits, it deals additional
damage equal to your Agility.

#### Double Quiver
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You can hold up to 40 arrows
in one quiver and it takes up
only 1 slot.

### Armor
For getting the most out of protective wear

#### Interposing Arm
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you wield a shield with

makes a weapon attack against
a creature adjacent to you, you
can use your reaction to cause
the damage to apply your shield
before the target.

#### Hurl Shield
**XP Cost: 1,000**
You can make a ranged weapon

attack is a Strength test that
is ranged 5 and deals 3 + S
damage on a tier 2 result and 6
+ S damage on a tier 3 result.

#### Wall of Protection
**XP Cost: 1,500**
While you have a shield

wielding a shield, you can use a

#### Shield Bash
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When get a tier 3 result on the
Knockback maneuver and force
move the target while you have
a shield equipped, the target is

#### Stalwart
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
While wearing armor, the
distance you can be force
moved is reduced by 1, and you
can’t be knocked prone by tier 1
and 2 attack results or by tier 2
and 3 RR results.

#### Sacrifice Armor
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When an attack reduces armor

damage left, you can reduce the
remaining damage to 0. If you
do, the armor reduced to 0 AD
is destroyed.

#### Immovable
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Creatures of your size can’t
force move you or knock you
prone with mundane attacks or
the Knockback maneuver while

#### Second Skin
**XP Cost: 2,000**
Armor you wear occupies 1
fewer slots.

#### Stand Strong
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
While you wear armor, you
gain a +1 bonus on Strength
RRs.

#### Headgear
**XP Cost: 1,000**
While you wear armor or a
magic item in your head slot,
you gain a +1 bonus on Mind
RRs.

#### Jury-Rig Repairs
**XP Cost: 1,500**
You can quickly repair a piece
of armor while you are not in
combat, restoring 1d6 AD to
it. You can’t use this trait again
until you finish a rest. This trait
stacks with Emergency Repairs
from the Blacksmithing tree.

#### Pierce This
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you take piercing
damage you can turn it into
regular damage (no action
required). Once you use this
trait, you can’t use it again until
you finish a rest.

Non-doom tier 1 results of

When you cast an benefaction

When you cast a benefaction

#### Delayed Inspiration
**XP Cost: 1,500**

#### Bard
**XP Cost: 2,000**

additional number of squares

stacks with Jumper.

#### Baleful Teleport
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you teleport a target
with a conjuration spell, you
can choose to have the target
take damage eqaul to twice
your Mind.

#### Portal Pocket
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You gain an additional
belt slot that exists in an
extradimensional space only
you can access. If you die, any
items in that slot disappear into
the void.

#### Summoning Ward
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
Creatures you summon with
conjuration spells have AD
equal to their power.

#### Blessed Summons
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you summon a creature
with a conjuration spell, they
are blessed.

#### Improved Summons
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast a conjuration
spell that allows you to
summon a monster, you can
summon a monster with power
equal to what the spell says plus
your Mind.

#### Telepathic Connection
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you summon a creature
with a conjuration spell, you
can telepathically communicate
with them and use their senses
as your own (while still using
yours) over any distance until
the spell ends.

### Elemental
For maximal use of elemental spells

#### Burn Baby
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast an elemental
spell that deals damage to
a target, the target takes
additional damage equal to your
Mind.

#### Lingering Fire
**XP Cost: 1,000**
At the start of your combat
turn, you can choose a creature
you dealt damage to with an
elemental spell on your last
turn (no action required). The
creature takes damage equal to
your Mind.

#### Fire Ward
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast an elemental
spell, you gain a fire ward that
lasts until the start of your next
combat turn. When a creature
deals damage to you with a
melee attack while the ward is
active, they take 1d10 damage.

#### Burned in the Brain
**XP Cost: 2,000**
As a rest activity, you can
absorb an elemental spellbook.
The book is destroyed, and you
always wield it. It occupies no
slots. If you use this rest activity
again, you lose previously
absorbed books.

#### Hurl the Storm
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast a ranged
elemental spell, the spell’s range
increases a number of squares
equal to your Mind.

#### Elemental Mastery
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Non-doom tier 1 results of
rank 0 and 1 conjuration spells
you cast don’t add to the chaos
count. Your conjuration spells
of rank 2 and higher are treated
as 2 ranks lower when they
trigger a backlash.

#### Selective Storm
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast an elemental
spell that affects all creatures
in an area of effect, you can
choose a number of creatures
equal to your Mind. Those
creatures aren’t targeted by the
spell.

#### Spreading Storm
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast an elemental
spell that creates an area of
effect, you can increase all
dimensions of the area of effect
by 1.

#### Water Shield
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast an elemental
spell, you can harness the magic
to surround your body in a
water ward and gain AD equal
to your Mind. The AD granted
by this trait disappears at the
end of a rest.

#### Water Cannon
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When a Huge or smaller
creature takes damage from an
elemental spell you cast, you
can slide the creature a number
of squares equal to your Mind
after the spell resolves.

#### Might of Waves
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you force move a
creature into an object with an
elemental spell or the Water
Cannon trait, the creature takes
additional damage equal to the
forced movement remaining
when they collide.

#### Split the Sea
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast an elemental
spell that has a target of 1
creature, object, ally, enemy,
or target, you can target one
additional qualifying target.

### Enchantment
For creating and using magic items

#### Material Transfer
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
As a rest activity, choose two
armor or weapon items you
can touch, such as iron heavy
armor and a steel sword. The
materials used in each item are
magically swapped.

#### Enchantransfer
**XP Cost: 1,000**
As a rest activity, choose two
weapons or two suits of armor
or two shields you can touch.
At least one item must be
magic. The magic properties
of each item are swapped with
the other.

#### Most of Magic
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you finish a rest, you
gain a magic charge for each
magic item worn in a slot.
You can spend a charge after
you make a test to give it a +1
bonus. Old charges disappear
when you gain new ones.

#### Energy Transfer
**XP Cost: 2,000**
As a rest activity, choose a
magic item you can touch and
an item you’re crafting. You
destroy the magic item, and
the other item gains a number
of crafting points equal to the
destroyed item’s crafting goal.

#### Twice Enchanted
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you work on crafting an
enchanting item as part of the
Craft Equipment rest activity,
you can make two crafting rolls
for the item or make a second
crafting roll for a different
enchanting item.

#### Enchanting Epiphany
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you make a crafting roll
for an enchanting item, you get
a crit when the dice without
modifiers equal 18, 19, or 20.

#### Lasting Items
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Enchanting items you create
that become useless when they
have no UD gain an additional
1 UD.

#### Five Steps Ahead
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you start crafing a new
enchanting item, the item
automatically gains crafting
points equal to 5d10 multiplied
by your Mind.

#### Hands for Tools
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
You don’t need tools to make
crafting rolls for alchemy,
blacksmithing, or enchanting
items.

#### Mind Protection
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you wear a magic item
in your head slot, you gain a +1
bonus to Mind RRs.

#### Strong Back
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you wear a magic item
in your waist slot, you gain a +1
bonus to Strength RRs.

#### Fast Feet
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you wear a magic item
in your feet slot, you gain a +1
bonus to Agility RRs.

### Illusion
For maximal use of illusion spells

#### Lasting Illusion
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast an illusion spell
with a duration listed in UD,
add an addtioinal 1 UD to the
duration.

#### Illusion Mastery
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Non-doom tier 1 results of rank
0 and 1 illusion spells you cast
don’t add to the chaos count.
Your illusion spells of rank
2 and higher are treated as 2
ranks lower when they trigger
a backlash.

#### Big Dreams
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast an illusion
with a size (such as Small or
Medium) the illusion’s size
increases by 1.

#### Split Illusion
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast an illusion spell
that has a target of 1 creature,
object, ally, enemy, or target,
you can target one additional
qualifying target.

#### Long-Distance Illusion
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast a ranged
illusion spell, the spell’s range
increases a number of squares
equal to your Mind.

#### Mind Shatter
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When a creature makes one
of your illusions created by an
illusion spell you cast disappear
by interacting with it, they take
1d6 damage.

#### You’re a Believer
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast an illusion spell
that targets a creature, you can
choose to take 1d6 P damage
before you roll to gain a +1
bonus on the casting against
that creature.

#### It’s Real
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast an illusion spell,
you can choose one mundane
object that is part of the illusion
that becomes real until the end
of the DT.

#### Free Light
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast an illusion
spell, you can choose to have an
object held or carried by your
or a willing ally glow with 0/5
light until the end of the DT.

#### Strong Glow
**XP Cost: 1,000**
The light granted by your Free
Light trait becomes 5/5.

#### Goodbye
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you activate your Free
Light trait, you can choose
to become invisible until the
end of your next combat turn
instead of creating light. This
invisibilty ends if you attack or
cast a spell.

#### Telepathic Connection
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you use your Goodbye
trait, you can choose another
creature within 5 squares
of your to become invisible
instead of you.

### Knowledge
For recalling lore and clever improvisation

#### Cram
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you read a lore book as
a rest activity, you can read a
second lore book as part of the
same activity.

#### Specific Research
**XP Cost: 1,000**
As a rest activity, you can read
a lore book. Instead of gaining
its normal benefits, you can ask
the Ref up to three questions
about the book’s subject, which
the Ref answers honestly if the
answer isn’t secret.

#### Shared Knowledge
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you read a lore book
as a rest activity to gain a skill
bonus, you can read the book
aloud. If you do, choose one
human who rests with you.
They gain the benefit too.

#### Memorization
**XP Cost: 2,000**
As a rest activity, choose a lore
book you have to study. You
gain its skill bonus and are
always wielding it even when
you don’t have it until you use
this rest activity again.

#### Schematics
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
You can sketch a room with a
journal, quill, and inkpot. As a
rest activity, you can study the
sketch. You gain a +2 bonus on
tests made to interact with the
room. It lasts until you finish
a rest.

#### Quick Reference
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you fail a Mind test to
recall lore and have a lore book
that relates to the lore, you can
attempt the test again. You can
use this trait a number of times
equal to your Mind, regaining
all uses when you finish a rest.

#### Expert Plan
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you take the Prepare for
Task rest activity and have a
journal, quill, and inkpot, you
can diagram your preparations
to share with your allies. Each
human who rests with you
gains the activity’s benefit.

#### Monster Expert
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you are wielding a
monster lore book and a
monster is in your line of
effect, you know the monster’s
Stamina, power, and the names
of their attacks and traits.

#### Improvised Equipment
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
As a rest activity, you create an
object that can be used as any
mundane item. After using the
object once to complete a task
(per the Ref), it is destroyed.
If the item has a duration, it’s
duration becomes 1 DT.

#### Labeled Inventory
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Each of your backpack slot’s
number is reduced by 1
(minimum 1), making it easier
to pull equipment from your
backpack.

#### Improvised Bomb
**XP Cost: 1,500**
As a rest activity, you can
make 1 fire bomb without any
materials or crafting rolls. The
bomb becomes useless at the
end of your next rest.

#### Special Item
**XP Cost: 2,000**
As a rest activity, choose a non-
weapon, non-armor mundane
item you have. You gain a +1
bonus on tests that reasonably
incorporate the use of this item
(per the Ref). The bonus lasts
until you use this activity again.

### Leverage
For getting the most out of your equipment

#### One More Spell
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
You can cast a spell from a
spellbook that has no UD
remaining. If you do, the
spellbook is destroyed after
casting the spell.

#### Recycling
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When a potion is consumed,
you can retrieve the vial that is
left behind. When you have 10
vials from the same potion, you
can collect the residue into one
vial to create a potion of that
type as a rest activity.

#### Organ Harvester
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you harvest crafting
materials from the corpse
of a creature, there is a 25%
chance you gain enough of the
component to craft two items.

#### Drink Fully
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You can consume a healing
potion as an action instead of
a maneuver. If you do, you
don’t roll dice to determine
how much Stamina you regain.
Instead, you gain the maximum
amount the dice allow.

#### More for Less
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you roll a UD for a piece
of mundane equipment that has
only 1 die remaining at the start
of rolling the item’s dice, then
you can only lose the UD on a
result of 1.

#### Break It
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you use a piece of
mundane equipment as part
of a test to overcome a goal
and get a non-doom result on
the test, you can destroy the
equipment to improve the
result of the test by 1 tier.

#### Half Portions
**XP Cost: 1,500**
You only need to eat half of a
ration’s worth of food during a
rest to get the benefit of resting.

#### Groundroll
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You don’t need a bedroll, bed,
or similar soft surface to sleep
on to get the benefit of resting.

#### Lasting Light
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you carry a mundane
light source, its UD maximum
increases by 1.

#### Torch Warrior
**XP Cost: 1,000**
You can wield a lit torch as a
melee weapon with a range of
melee 1 that deals 3 + A or S
damage on a tier 2 result and
6 + A or S damage on a tier 3
result.

#### Lights Out
**XP Cost: 1,500**
If you damage another creature
with a torch attack, you can
choose to have the torch be
extinquished and destroyed
in the attack. The damage is
doubled.

#### Fire Bomb
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You can use a lit lantern like a
fire bomb. The lantern and its
oil are destroyed after being
used this way.

### Necromancy
For maximal use of necromancy spells

#### Soul Absorption
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast a necromancy
spell that kills a creature, you
gain AD equal to your Mind.
This AD disappears when you
finish a rest.

#### Many Curses
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you cast a necromancy
spell and get a tier 3 result that
makes the spell’s target boned,
they gain 1 additional boned
level.

#### You’ll Suffer
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast a necromancy
spell that targets a creature,
you can choose to take 1d6
P damage before you roll to
gain a +1 bonus on the casting
against that creature.

#### Sacrifice
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast a necromancy
spell that deals damage to
creature, you can choose to take
1d6 P damage to deal additional
damage with the spell equal to
the damage you take.

#### Distant Necromancy
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast a ranged
necromancy spell, the spell’s
range increases a number of
squares equal to your Mind.

#### Necromancy Mastery
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Non-doom tier 1 results of
rank 0 and 1 necromancy spells
you cast don’t add to the chaos
count. Your necromancy spells
of rank 2 and higher are treated
as 2 ranks lower when they
trigger a backlash.

#### Blessings for a Curse
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast a necromancy
spell that targets a creature,
you can remove a combined
total of 2 levels of blessed from
you and any willing ally within
5 squares of you to make the
target boned.

#### Split Necromancy
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast a necromancy
spell that has a target of 1
creature, object, ally, enemy,
or target, you can target one
additional qualifying target.

#### Undead Fortitude
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you cast a necromancy
spell, you don’t need to breathe
and can’t suffocate until the end
of the DT. If you are already
under this effect, you can
choose a willing ally within 5
squares of you to have it.

#### Flow Like Blood
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you cast a necromancy
spell, you have an amorphous
body and gear until the end of
the DT. While in this state, you
can fit through openings that
are at least 1 inch square.

#### Demon’s Sight
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you cast a necromancy
spell, you treat dim light and
darkness as if they were bright
light until the end of the DT.

#### Angelic Presence
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you cast a necromancy
spell, enemies who are within 2
squares of you take a -1 penalty
to their RRs until the end of
the DT.

### Pets
For raising and commanding animals

#### Buddy
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
As a rest activity, choose a
number of pets you own equal
to your Mind (minimum 1).
You gain a +2 bonus on Mind
tests made to interact with
those pets until you use the
activity again.

#### Pack Animals
**XP Cost: 1,000**
The Stamina maximum of each
pet affected by your Buddy trait
is raised by 4 multiplied by the
number of pets affected by your
Buddy trait.

#### Move Together
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you use the Command
maneuver, you can take the
Move Speed maneuver as part
of the same maneuver.

#### Beastheart
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you take the Command
maneuver, you can command
up to two pets you own.

#### Tricks
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
While you own a pet, they
gain a +1 bonus to one of
the following skills: Climb,
Endurance, Hide, Jump, Lift,
Search, Sneak, or Swim. This
trait stacks with Extra Tricks.

#### Travel Animal
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you make a test related
to the forager, guide, or scout
travel role, you can have one
pet you own help you. The test
gains a +1 bonus.

#### Extra Tricks
**XP Cost: 1,500**
While you own a pet, they
gain a +1 bonus to one of
the following skills: Climb,
Endurance, Hide, Jump, Lift,
Search, Sneak, or Swim. This
trait stacks with Tricks.

#### Share Food
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You can stack rations and
animal feed in the same slot.
You can eat animal feed and
pets you car for can can eat
rations.

#### Mounted Speed
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
While you ride a pet you own,
their speed is increased by 1.

#### Dungeon Critter
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Squeezing doesn’t halve the
speed of or grant a bonus to
attack the pets you own.

#### Charge
**XP Cost: 1,500**
If you hit a target with a melee
weapon attack while riding a
pet you own after they move
at least 2 squares on the same
turn, the attack gains a +2
bonus to damage.

#### Beast of Burden
**XP Cost: 2,000**
At the end of a rest, choose one
pet you own. This pet gains
two additional backpack slots.
The additional slots disappear
at the end of your next rest.

### Reputation
For getting the most out of your village

#### Call Daddy
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
Choose a merchant in your
village. When you need to
roll for an item’s availability
from that merchant, the AO
increases by 5%. You can
change your choice of merchant
once every cycle.

#### Also Mommy
**XP Cost: 1,000**
Your Call Daddy trait can apply
to two merchants of your
choice. You can change one or
both once every cycle.

#### Call Grandaddy
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Your Call Daddy trait increase
the AO chances by 10% instead
of 5%.

#### And Grandmommy
**XP Cost: 2,000**
Your Call Daddy trait applies to
three merchants of your choice.
You can change up to three
once every cycle.

#### Discount
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
Choose a merchant in your
village. You can purchase items
in that merchant’s shop for 5%
cheaper. You can change your
choice of merchant once every
cycle.

#### Here’s One for Free
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you purchase 5 or more
a single item that is destroyed
or useless after being used (such
as a torch, ration, or potion)
from the merchant affected by
your Discount trait, you get
one of that item for free.

#### Double Discount
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Your Discount trait can apply
to two merchants of your
choice. You can change one or
both once every cycle.

#### On Sale
**XP Cost: 2,000**
Items affect by your Discount
trait are 10% cheaper for you
instead of 5%.

#### Favorite Customer
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
Choose a merchant who crafts
items in your village. When
that merchant crafts an item
for you, they make two crafting
rolls a day instead of one. You
can change your choice of
merchant once every cycle.

#### VIP Treatment
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When a merchant affected by
your Favorite Customer trait
begins crafting an item for you,
the item starts with 10% of its
crafting points.

#### Have it in the Back
**XP Cost: 1,500**
You can ask the merchant
affected by your Favorite
Customer trait to start crafting
an item you don’t have the
materials for. There’s a 20%
they have the materials and
charge you 10% for the work.

#### Building Efficiency
**XP Cost: 2,000**
The merchant affected by
your Favorite Customer trait
operates as if they were 1 level
higher than their actual level.

### Slashing
For mastery of knives, swords, greatswords, and glaives

#### Finesse the Blade
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
You can use Agility instead
of Strength when making an
attack and dealing damage with
a sword.

#### Swift Block
**XP Cost: 1,000**
You can add your Agility to the
Parry quality of any slashing
weapon you wield.

#### Knife Chucker
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you wield a knife, it’s
non-melee range increases by
2 squares.

#### Double Knife
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you are wielding two
knives, you can throw both as
a ranged attack, dealing double
damage to the target on a hit.

#### Quickstep
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you use the Shift
maneuver while wielding a
slashing weapon, you can move
2 squares instead of 3.

#### Dance Away
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you counter, you can
take the Shift maneuver as part
of the reaction while you are
wielding a slashing weapon.

#### Follow Up
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you push a target with
the Knockback maneuver while
wielding a slashing weapon,
you can move into any space
they vacated without provoking
opportunity attacks as part of
the maneuver.

#### On the Move
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you deal damage to
another creature with a melee
slashing weapon attack you
can move 1 square without
provoking opportunity attacks.

#### Swordplay
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
You can add your Strength to
the Parry quality of any slashing
weapon you wield.

#### Interceding Blade
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you wield a slashing
weapon and a creature makes
a weapon attack against a
creature adjacent to you, you
can use your reaction to cause
the damage to apply your
weapon before the target.

#### Break the Blade
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When an attack reduces a
slashing weapon you wield
to 0 AD and there is still
damage left, you can reduce the
remaining damage to 0. If you
do, the weapon is destroyed.

#### Scabard
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You gain an additional belt
slot that can only hold slashing
weapons.

### Stabbing
For mastery of stilettos, spears, warpicks, and pikes

#### Javelin
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
You can make a ranged attack
with a spear with a range of
range 5.

#### Grappling Spear
**XP Cost: 1,000**
If you attach your spear to
a rope, you can use it like a
grappling hook.

#### Get Over Here
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you hit with a ranged
attack with a stabbing weapon
attached to a rope and they
are your size or smaller and
not further from you than the
rope’s length, you can pull the
target 1 square towards you.

#### Stabbing Holster
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You gain an additional belt slot
that can only hold stabbing
weapons.

#### Bury the Point
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you hit a creature with a
melee stabbing weapon attack,
you can deal twice as much
damage as you normally would,
but the weapon becomes
useless until you use a rest
activity to fix it.

#### Spinning Shaft
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you get a tier 3 result
on a melee stabbing weapon
attack, choose another target
within the weapon’s distance.
That target takes damage equal
to your Strength.

#### Nope
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you hit with an
opportunity attack made with
a stabbing weapon, the target’s
speed is reduced to 0 until the
end of the current combat turn.

#### Wind Up
**XP Cost: 2,000**
While you are wielding a
stabbing weapon, you can use a
maneuver to prepare an attack.
If you make a melee attack with
the stabbing weapon this turn,
it deals additional damage equal
to your Strength.

#### Stabathon
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you score a crit with a
stabbing weapon, any other
attacks you make one the same
turn gain a +2 bonus.

#### Triple Crit
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you score a crit with
a stabbing weapon, you deal
thriple the weapon’s tier 3
damage instead of double.

#### Stabbing Crit
**XP Cost: 1,500**
You score a crit on stabbing
weapon attacks when the dice
without modifiers equal 18, 19,
or 20.

#### Improved Stab
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You score a crit on stabbing
weapon attacks when the dice
without modifiers equal 17, 18,
19, or 20.

### Thievery
For breaking, entering, hiding, and sneaking

#### Sieze the Advantage
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you hit with a weapon
attack using Agility that has
bonuses outside of skills
and characteristics, you deal
additional damage equal to
twice those bonuses.

#### From the Shadows
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you make a weapon
attack against a target you are
hidden from using Agility, the
attack gains a +3 bonus instead
the normal +2 bonus for being
hidden.

#### Dark Senses
**XP Cost: 1,500**
While you are not wearing
armor and not wielding a
shield, you treat dim light and
darkness as if they were bright
light until the end of the DT.

#### Seeing Things
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you get a tier 1 or 2
result a test made to hide from
or sneak by another creature
wile you are not wearing
armor, you can reroll the test
but must use the new result.

#### Safe Cracking
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you use lock picks on
a test made to pick a lock, you
can roll the test twice and
choose either result.

#### Into the Pack
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you use the Move Speed
maneuver, you can use the Pick
Up Item maneuver too. You
don’t need to have a free hand
slot and can put the item in any
free slot when you use the Pick
Up Item maneuver.

#### Out of the Pack
**XP Cost: 1,500**
Each of your backpack slot’s
number is reduced by 1
(minimum 1).

#### Bursting Purse
**XP Cost: 2,000**
You can carry an additional 500
gc in a coin purse.

#### Stealthy
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When make a test to hide
or sneak while you are not
wearing armor, you can roll
the test twice and choose either
result.

#### Above It All
**XP Cost: 1,000**
You gain a +2 bonus to tests
made to climb, jump, swim, or
do gymnastics while you are
not wearing armor.

#### Dodgy
**XP Cost: 1,500**
You gain a +2 bonus on Agility
RRs while you are not wearing
armor.

#### Tumble
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you take damage while
not wearing armor, you can
use your reaction to halve the
damage. You can use this trait a
number of times equal to your
Agility and then must finish a
rest before your use it again.

### Travel
For fast, safe, and profitable overland travel

#### I Spy
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you make a test related
to the scout travel role while
carrying a spyglass, you can roll
the test twice and choose either
result.

#### Fade Away
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you are in the scout
travel role and carrying a spy
glass and encounter other
creatures while traveling, you
can begin the encounter hidden
from those creatures.

#### Must Go Faster
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you are in the scout
travel role and carrying a spy
glass and encounter other
creatures while traveling, you
and each creature in your party
can take 2d6 P damage to avoid
the encounter before it starts.

#### Ambush
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you get a tier 3 result on
a test related to the scout travel
role while carrying a spyglass,
any creatures you encounter
while traveling are surprised by
you and your allies.

#### Orienteering
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you make a test related
to the guide travel role while
carrying a compass, you can roll
the test twice and choose either
result.

#### A Path for All
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When are in the guide travel
role while carrying a compass,
any test you make that grants
a bonus to other travel roles
has that bonus increase by your
Mind.

#### Push It Real Good
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you are in the guide
or leader role and carrying a
compass or musical instrument,
you and each ally traveling with
you adds your Mind to tests
related to pushing to travel
further.

#### This Way
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you make a test related
to the guide travel role while
carrying a compass that doesn’t
result in the group getting
or remaining lost, you and
each creature traveling with
increases their pace by 3.

#### Foraging Expert
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you make a test related
to the forager travel roll and
gain at least 1 ration, you gain
1 additional ration. This trait
stacks with Food Finder.

#### Hunting Expert
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you make a test related
to the guide travel role while
carrying a bow weapon and
ammunition for it, you can roll
the test twice and choose either
result.

#### Food Finder
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you make a test related
to the forager travel roll and
gain at least 1 ration, you gain
1 additional ration. This trait
stacks with Foraging Expert.

#### All You Can Eat
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you make a test related
to the guide travel role while
carrying a bow weapon and
ammunition for it, you gain 7
rations instead of 1d6 + 1.

### Unarmed
For mastery of kicks, punches, headbutts, and improvised weapons

#### Throw Stuff
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you use an improvised
weapon that occupies 1 slot, it
has a range of melee 1/range 5.

#### Everything’s a Mace
**XP Cost: 1,000**
When you use an improvised
weapon, ask the Ref if it most
resembles a hammer, mace,
maul, or flail. The improvised
weapon uses the same stats
except that it has no weapon
qualities.

#### Break Stuff
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you hit with a melee
attack with an improvised
weapon, you can break the
weapon and deal additional
damage equal to your Agility or
Strength (your choice).

#### Bashing Benefits
**XP Cost: 2,000**
Any improvised weapon you
wield counts as a bashing
weapon and has the Pummeling
quality.

#### Pack a Punch
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
When you get a tier 3 result
with an unarmed or improvised
weapon attack, the attack deals
additional damage equal to your
Agility or Strength.

#### Wrestle Big
**XP Cost: 1,000**
While you have at least 1
hand slot free, you can target
creatures 1 size larger than
you or smaller with the Grab
maneuver.

#### Hard to Pin
**XP Cost: 1,500**
While you have at least 1 hand
slot free and use the Escape
Grab maneuever, you can roll
twice and choose either result
and if you get a tier 3 result,
you can counter the grabber as
part of your escape.

#### Pack a Kick
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you get a tier 2 result
with an unarmed or improvised
weapon attack, the attack deals
additional damage equal to your
Agility or Strength.

#### Extra Push
**XP Cost: 500 (Starting)**
While you have at least 1 hand
slot free and push a target with
the Knockback maneuver, you
can push them 1 additional
square.

#### Big Push
**XP Cost: 1,000**
While you have at least 1
hand slot free, you can target
creatures 1 size larger than you
or smaller with the Knockback
maneuver.

#### To the Walls
**XP Cost: 1,500**
When you force move a
creature into an object while
you have at least 1 hand
slot free, the creature takes
additional damage equal to the
forced movement remaining
when they collide.

#### You Are My Weapon
**XP Cost: 2,000**
When you force move a
creature into another while you
have at least 1 hand slot free,
both creatures take damage
equal to the forced movement
remaining when they collide.

## Equipment
An adventurer’s equipment is essential to their
survival. Torches, swords, spellbooks, rations,
and more make the difference between a big
payday and your last day ever.

Inventory Cards
Items that you can hold in your inventory are
detailed on cards that are included with this

game. These cards contain the item’s game

statistics as shown on this page.

Marking Cards
There isn’t a card included for every variation
of an item within the game. For example,
alchemist’s tools and masterwork alchemist’s
tool use the same card, as do an iron sword and
a steel sword. If you’re using a digital version of
the game, you can copy and modify cards or print
them out and write on them as you like.
If you have a physical copy of this game [Playtest
note: no one does yet], clear plastic card sleeves
were included with it. An inventory card fits
inside of a sleeve and you can write on the sleeve
with dry erase marker. All physical versions
include the digital files, so you can also print out
more cards as needed.

Blank Cards
Blank cards are included with the game to allow
the Ref to create their own equipment as needed.

Money
The currency of Cornath is gold coins (gc). An
inventory slot can hold 1 purse that holds up to
500 gc (or just a lose 500 gc).

Shopping
The equipment the PCs can buy depends on the
level of the merchants in their village. See Village
for more information.

Gear
Gear is a term that encompasses all the mundance
equipment crows use that doesn’t include armor
or weapons. A compass, a grappling hook, and
even a bedroll—it’s all gear.

Fine and Masterwork
Some pieces of gear have a fine and masterwork
version indicated on their card, each being an
improved version of the standard. The full price
of the fine and masterwork versions are listed
in parentheses next to the description of their
improved statistics. Other than price, the fine
and masterwork statistics stack with the item’s
standard statistics, but not with each other.

[Playtest Note: Information about hirelings and

vehicles will be included in future playtests.]

## Armor
You can only benefit from a shield’s AD by
wielding it. You can only benefit from a suit
of armor’s AD by wearing it. To wear a suit of
armor, it must be in your backpack slots. You can
only wear one suit of armor at a time. You can’t
don or doff a suit of armor when you are not in a
situation tracked in combat rounds.

Armor Types

There are four types of armor, each with its own
inventory card:

- Shields: Metal shields of all shapes
- Light: Cloth, hide, and leather
- Medium: Chain and scale mail
- Heavy: Plate, ring, and splint mail

Armor Enchantments
Armor enchantments can be added to suits of
armor or shields. The Armor Enchantments table
shows the Enchanting skill bonus, materials, and
crafting goal required to enchant the armor and if
the enchantment can be placed on a shield, suit of
armor, or both. It also shows the price for buying
the enchantment. If you buy armor with an
enchantment on it, add this price to the armor’s
base price.

If a suit or armor or a shield has a single

enchantment that requires no more than a +1
Enchanting bonus to craft, it can recieve a second
and different echantment that requires no more
than +1 Enchanting bonus to craft. Otherwise
only mundane armo rcan get an enchantment.

Some armor has unique enchantments that aren’t
listed here. The information for crafting that
armor is listed on the item’s card.

The following enchantments require a +1
Enchanting bonus to craft:

- Demon’s Head: A demon’s head is emblazoned
on both sides of this shield. While you wield it,
you deal additional damage with a counter equal
to your Agility or Strength (your choice) as the
outer face bites your target. When you take
damage from a counter, you take an additional 1
damage as the inner face bites you.
- Feather: While you wear this armor, whenever
you fall, the damage is reduced by 3d6. When
you are force moved, you can be moved 1

additional square.

- Glow: While you wear or wield this armor,
you can use a maneuver to make it glow, giving
off light 5/5. This effect has 2 UD and the UD
recharge when you finish a rest. Once you turn
this effect on, it can’t be turned off until the
UD expire. If you roll a doom while wearing,
wielding, or carrying the armor and the light
effect is available but hasn’t been turned off, the
effect activates.

- Heavy: While you wear this armor and are
force moved, you reduce the move by 1. If you
are knocked prone, you must use your action to
stand up instead of a maneuver.
- Luring: When another creature hits you with
a melee attack while you wear or wield this
armor, you can use a reaction to slide them 1

square. If a creature gets a crit against you with

an attack, you can’t use this enchantment’s
reaction until the end of the DT and the
creature can slide you 1 square.
- Silent: While wearing this armor, you gain a
+1 bonus to tests made to hide and sneak. If
you get a tier 1 result on a test made to hide or
sneak, you are boned.
- Slick: This armor secretes an non-flammable
oil. While wearing it, you can move 1 additional
square when you use the Shift maneuver
and you gain a +1 bonus to the Escape Grab
maneuver’s test. When you roll a doom on an
Agility or Strength test, you fall prone.
- Spell-Storing: As a rest activity or as part of
the Repair Armor rest activity while repairing
this armor, you can touch a spellbook to it.
While wear or wield the armor, you can cast the
spell as if you were holding the spellbook. If you
roll a doom on any test while wearing, wielding,
or carrying the armor, the spell is immediately
cast, and the Ref picks the targets. If there are
no viable targets, add 1d6 to the chaos count
and the spell is expended from the armor. Once
you cast the spell, it is gone from the armor.
The armor can only store one spell at a time.
When a spell is placed in it, and old spells are
gone from the armor.
- Telepathic Node: As a rest activity while
wearing this armor, you can choose up to five
other willing humans who rest with you to
become connected to the armor until you use
this rest activity again or die. While you wear
this armor, any of the creatures connected
to the armor can communicate telepathically
with each other and you as long as they have
line of effect to you and you can communicate
telepathically with them regardless of distance
or obstacles. If a creature connected to the
armor dies, you and the other creatures
connected take 1d10 P damage. If you die,
connected creatures take 2d10 P damage.

- Waterwalking: While wearing this armor,
you can walk on the surface of any liquid as if
it were solid ground. If you fall prone while
standing on liquid, this armor’s enchantment
ceases to function until the end of the DT and
you take a -2 penalty on a tests made to swim.

The following enchantments require a +2

Enchanting bonus to craft:

- Banishing: When a creature hits you with
a melee attack while you wear or wield this
armor, you can use a reaction to push them
1d6 squares. If a creature gets a crit on a melee
attack against you while you wear or wield this
armor, you fly vertically up 1d6 x 10 feet and
then fall and can’t use your reaction to activate
this armor in response to the attack.
- Climbing: While you wear this armor, you
gain a climbing speed equal to your walking
speed. If you are knocked prone while
climbing, you immediately fall and this armor’s
enchantment ceases to function until the end of
the DT.
- Dancing: As a maneuver while wielding this
shield, you command it to float in the air and
follow and protect you, freeing it from your
hand slot though you are still considered
wielding it, until the end of the DT. A creature
who scores a crit against you while the shield
is floating can grab it and wield it (no action
required), causing the shield to stop floating.
The shield can’t be made to float again until you
finish a rest. You can only have one dancing
shield active at a time.
- Deep: While wearing this armor, you gain a
swimming speed equal to your walking speed
and can’t suffocate. When you fall while
wearing this armor, the fall damage increases
by 1d6.
- Flying: As a maneuver while wielding this
shield, you can place it on the ground and make
it hover. While hovering, a Medium or smaller
creature can ride on the shield and gains a flying
speed equal to their walking speed and counts
as wielding the shield. The shield can fly until
the end of the DT or until it loses all of its AD,
at which point it and any creature using it to
fly falls. Any attacks or damaging effects made
against the creature flying that originate from
below first damage the shield before any other
armor the creature wears. The shield can’t be
made to fly again until you finish a rest.

- Passthrough: While you wear this armor,
you use a maneuver to move up to your speed.
While you move, mundane walls and other
obstructions can’t hamper your movement.
If you end this move inside of a solid object,
you are shunted back to the space where you
entered it and take 1d6 P damage for each space
you are moved through on your way back. This
maneuver has a 1 UD that must be rolled after
activating the effect. The UD recharge when
you finish a rest.
- Revenge: When you take damage while
wearing this armor it gains 1d6 charges. When
you hit a creature with a melee attack, you can
expend all charges built up in the armor and
deal additional damage equal to the charges
spent. If the charges reach 12 or higher, they
are all expended and you take P damage equal
to the charges spent. All charges in the armor
disappear at the end of a DT.

- Speedy: While you wear this armor, your speed
increases by 1. If an attack against you is a crit,
your speed is reduced to 0 until the end of your
next combat turn.
- Sustaining: While you wear this armor, you
don’t need to eat to avoid starvation or gain the
benefits of a rest. If you die while wearing this
armor, it consumes any items you have in your
hand and belt slots when you die.
- Victory: When you kill another creature while
wearing this armor, you are blessed. If you have
a wound while wearing this armor, you can’t be
blessed by any effect.

## Weapons
A weapon’s equipment card shows how much
damage it deals on a tier 2 or 3 result with an
attack. It doesn’t show the tier 1 result, which is
always a miss.

Weapon Types
A weapon’s type determines the skill that can be
applied to attacks made with it. A weapon can a
be bashing, bow, chopping, slashing, stabbing, or
unarmed weapon.

Weapon Qualities
A weapon can have any of the following qualities:

- Brutal: When you get a crit on an attack with
this weapon, you deal twice as much damage
with the attack as your normally would.
- Cumbersome: The weapon only occupies 1 slot
on your backpack or belt but requires 2 hand
slots to wield.

- Disengage: When you take the Shift maneuver
while wielding this weapon, you can move 1
additional square as part of the maneuver. If
you wield two weapons with this quality, the
benefits stack.
- Dismember: When you score a crit on an
attack made with this weapon, it removes one
of the target’s limbs. Roll on the Dismember
table to determine the limb removed.
- Light: When you hit with a melee attack while
weilding two light weapons, you deal additonal
damage equal to the unused weapon’s tier 2
result (without the inclusion of your Agility or
Strength as part of the damage). While a hand
slot is empty, it counts as a light weapon that
can be used to make unarmed strikes.
- Parry X: While you wield this weapon, you
can use it to absorb damage as if it were a shield.
The weapon has AD equivalent to X. When the
weapon’s AD is 0, it takes a -1 damage penalty.
You can repair the weapon as if it were a shield
using the Repair Armor rest activity.
- Pummeling: When you get a tier 3 result
with an attack made with this weapon against
a creature who is your size or smaller, you can
push them 1 square. When you get a crit on an
attack with this weapon, you can knock a target
of your size or smaller prone.
- Reload: You must use a maneuver to load 1
piece of ammunition into this weapon before it

can be used to attack. After being used to attack,
it must be loaded again before it can be used to
make another attack.

Dismember
| d6 | Limb and Effect* |
| --- | --- |
| 1-2 | Arm: The attacker chooses which arm |
is removed. Any object held in the arm
is dropped and any creature grabbed
by the arm is no longer grabbed. If
the creature uses the arm as part of a
natural weapon attack (such as with
claws) the attack takes a -1 damage
penalty. If the creature has no arms
remaing, they can’t make any attacks or
complete taskts that require arms.
| 3-4 | Leg: The attacker chooses which leg |
| --- | --- |
is removed. The creature’s speed is
reduced proportionally. If the creature
has no legs remaining, they can’t stand
and their speed is 0.

| 5 | Arm or Leg: The attacker chooses to |
| --- | --- |
remove an arm or leg of their choice.

| 6 | Head: The creature dies. |
| --- | --- |
*Some creatures have only legs. Others have
limbs that serve as both arms and legs. When
you remove a limb from such a creature, the
Ref determines which effects on the table are
appropriate, combining or adjusting as they
see fit. If a creature doesn’t have discernable
anatomy, you instead deal twice as much damage
against them as you normally would with a crit
rather than dismembering them.

Upgraded Weapons

unarmed can have their damage increased by
using better metal to create them. Bows can have
their range increased by using better wood in
their construction. The weapon upgrade tables
below show each upgrade’s bonus and price.

goal required to make weapons and ammunition
from these materials.

Weapon Enchantments
Weapon enchantments can be added to existing
weapons. The Weapon Enchantments table
shows the Enchanting skill bonus, materials, and
crafting goal required to enchant a weapon. It
also shows the price for adding an echantment
to a weapon. If you buy a weapon with an
enchantment, add its price to the weapon’s price.

If a weapon has a single enchantment that
requires no more than a +1 Enchanting bonus
to craft, it can recieve a second and different
echantment that requires no more than +1
Enchanting bonus to craft. Otherwise only a
mundane weapon can get an enchantment.
Some weapons have unique enchantments that
aren’t listed here. The information for crafting
those weapons are listed on the items’ cards.
Unarmed weapons can’t be enchanted.

The following enchantments require a +1
Enchanting bonus to craft:

- Absorbing: When you get a crit on an attack
with this weapon against a creature, you can
choose one attack from the creature’s stat block
and make that attack using the creature’s stats
this turn. When you get a doom on an attack
made with this weapon, you can’t make attacks

with it until the end of your next combat turn.

- Defending: The gains the Parry 5 quality or
if it already has it, it increases by 5. When the
weapon’s AD is 0, it takes a -2 damage penalty.
- Exploding: When you kill a creature with this
weapon, they explode. Each creature within 1
square of them takes 1d6 damage. If the creature

already explodes on death, any damage they deal

is increased by 1d6. When you get a doom on

an attack with this weapon, a mundane item

you carry (chosen by the Ref) is destroyed.

- Impact: When you get a tier 3 result on an
attack with this weapon and the target isn’t
force moved, you can push the target 1 square.
If the target is already force moved by the
attack, you can force move them 1 additional
square. If you get a doom on an attack with this
weapon, the Ref slides you 1d10 squares.
- Lightning: When you hit a target with an
attack with this weapon, you can choose a
second target within 3 squares of the first. That
creature also takes the attack’s damage. This
effect has 1 UD rolled on use and recharges on
rest. When the UD are 0, you are boned.
- Poisoning: As a maneuver, you can command
this weapon to drip poison, an effect that has
1 UD to be rolled on use and which recharges
on rest. If the next attack you make with your
weapon is a hit, the target is boned. If you miss
with your next attack, you are boned.
- Raging: When you kill another creature with
this weapon, you can use a reaction to enter a
rage that lasts until the end of the DT. While
in the rage, you gain a +2 bonus to damage on
attacks with this weapon and a +1 bonus to
speed while you wield this weapon. When the
rage ends, you are boned.
- Slaying: You choose a specific creature type
when you place this enchantment on a weapon.
The weapon gains a +1 bonus on attacks against
creatures of that type and a -1 penalty on attacks
against all other creatures. You can only place
one slaying enchatment on a weapon.

- Sworn Foe: As a maneuver while wielding
this weapon, you can choose a creature within
10 squares of you. You deal an additional 2
damage on attacks with this weapon against
that creature. If that creature counters you, you
take 2 additional damage. If you choose a new
creature, the effects of this weapon no longer
apply to the old creature.

- Vicious: When you get a crit on an attack

with this weapon, you deal an additional 1d10
damage. When you get a doom on an attack
with this weapon, you take 1d10 P damage.

The following enchantments require a +2
Enchanting bonus to craft:

- Dancing: As a maneuver while wielding this

weapon, you command it to float in the air and

follow and fight for you, freeing it from your

hand slot(s) though you are still considered

wielding it, until the end of the DT. A creature
who scores a crit against you while the weapon
is floating can grab it and wield it (no action
required), causing the weapon to stop floating.
If the weapon is ever more than 1 square from
you, it stops floating. The weapon can’t be made
to float again until you finish a rest. You can
only have one dancing weapon active at a time.
- Flaming: As a maneuver while wielding this
weapon, you can command it to ignite in a
magical fire that gives the weapon a +2 damage
bonus and causes it to shed light 5/5. You can
use another maneuver to extinguish the flames.
When you get a doom on an attack with this
weapon, you take 1d10 damage as the weapon
suddenly bursts with flame and then its fire goes
out and you can’t ignite it again until the end of
the DT.
- Frosty: When you get a tier 3 result on an
attack with this weapon against a creature, the
creature’s speed is reduced by 1. This effect
can’t reduce a creature’s speed below 2. When
you get a doom result on an attack with this
weapon, your speed is reduced to 2 (if it isn’t
already lower) until the end of the DT.
- Gashing: When you get a tier 3 result on an
attack with this weapon against a creature, the
creature gains a bleeding gash. The gash deals
1d6 P damage to a creature at the start of their
combat turns. If target recieves magic healing
or the gash’s damage result is 6, the gash stops
dealing damage to the target. When get a doom
on an attack with the weapon, you get a gash.

Weapon Enchantments

- Hewing: This enchantment can only be added
to non-bow weapons. As an action, you can use
this weapon to make a hole in a wall, floor, or
ceiling that has a circumference of 1d10 feet and
a depth of 1d10 feet. This effect has 1 UD rolled
on use and recharges on rest. When the UD are
0, you are boned.
- Hungry: When you kill a creature with this
weapon, you gain 3 AD. AD gained this way
disappears at the end of the DT. If you wield
the weapon in a combat encounter and the
encounter ends without you having killed
anything with it, you take 1d6 P damage.
- Infinity: This enchantment can only be added
to bow weapons. If you don’t load aumminition
into this weapon, it generates its own phantom
ammunition that can be used to make attacks.
The ammunition has a -1 damamage penalty.

- Returning: This enchantment can only be
added to non-bow weapons. The weapon’s
ranged attack range increases by 5 and after
you use it to make a ranged attack, the weapon
return to your hand slot(s). If you get a doom

- Teleporting: When you deal damage to
another creature with this weapon, it gains
1d6 charges. As a maneuver, you can expend
all charges from it and teleport up to a number
of squares equal to the charges spent. If
the charges reach 12 or higher, they are all
expended and you take P damage equal to
the charges spent. All charges in the armor
disappear at the end of a DT.
- Weakening: When you hit a creature with a
tier 3 attack made with this weapon and do so
again before the end of your next combat turn,
the creature is boned. When you get a doom on
an attack with this weapon, you are boned.

Crafting Materials
Crafting materials (such as iron bars and demon
hides) have one generic inventory card that
comes in different sizes to allow for different
stack limits. Most crafting materials, particularly
monster body parts, can be found on adventures,
but some are also available for purchase as shown
on the Crafting Materials for Sale table.

Crafting Materials for Sale

| Material | Size | Price |

| Archmage obsidian bar | Small | 25 gc |
| Archmage willow log | Medium | 25 gc |
| Iron bar | Small | 5 gc |
| Hickory log | Medium | 5 gc |
| Necromancer deathtree log Medium | 50 gc |
| Necromancer silver bar | Small | 50 gc |
| Star diamond bar | Small | 100 gc |
| Starwood log | Medium | 100 gc |

Treassure

Mundane treasure that isn’t coins is divided into

two categories: art objects (jewelry, paintings,

sculptures, etc.) and gems (diamonds, emeralds,
rubies, etc.). Both have generic cards in the set
that allow you to write the item’s specific name
and value. Art object come in different sizes. The
Ref determines the value of a gem or art object.
Advice and examples can be found in The Ref.

Pets
Pets are domesticated animals who follow a
single human owner. You own pets your buy.

You can give a pet you own to another willing
human who becomes their owner. If you find
a domesticated animal with no owner, you can
become their owner with the following test.
2d10 + M + Handle Animal skill

1 The animal refuses to engage with you.
2 The animal follows you at a distance
for the next 24 hours, but doesn’t heed your

commands. If you spend your next rest activity
bonding with them, you become the owner
when you finish the rest.
3 You become the animal’s owner.

Pet Stats
A pet has backpack inventory slots that can be
used to carry equipment for their owner. A pet
comes outfitted with a special harness that holds
equipment and if the animal is Large or larger, a
saddle and other equipment required to ride it.
These backpack inventory slots take wounds just
like a PC’s.
You can ride any pet who is larger than you. You

occupy 6 of a creature’s inventory slots when you
ride them.
Pets need to eat each day just like PCs and must
eat during a rest to get the benefits of a rest.
While traveling in the wild, a pet can hunt and

forage for themselves, but in a dungeon or other
place with no food sources, they must consume
animal feed (the equivalent of rations for them).
Pets are not equipment.

Barding

Pets can wear armor, provided they have the

backpack slots to hold it. Barding for a pet costs
requires multiplying the base cost of the armor
multiplied by a number found on the Pet Barding
table based on the pet’s size. The pet’s size also

modifies the number of inventory slots the armor

takes up.

Pet Barding

| Pet Size | Price Modifier | Slots Occupied |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Medium | ×2 | +0 |
| Large | ×4 | +2 |
| Huge | ×8 | +4 |
