## The Village
Crows die and retire, but their village remains.
With Cornath recovering from near destruction,
your village is one of the only places left for
humans to survive. Invest in your village, and
your community will thrive and better prepare
you for delving into ruins. If you ignore your
village, it will decay, leaving you without a place
to buy equipment, sell treasure, or safely surive
your time outside of dungeons.

In Cornath every village is contained within a

ruin. These structures are enclosed, protecting
the people within from the Miasma and the
monsters wandering the wilds.

Other Villages

As you travel from one dungeon to another, you

might stop in a village other than your own to
rest or trade. These villages function the same

way as your own, except you can’t invest in them
and the Ref doesn’t have to worry about tracking
their prosperity or events. The Ref determines
the level of each instution in another village.

If you want to invest in more than one village,
ask the Ref. You’ll need to start tracking

Prosperity, events, and all the other stats for that
village yourself. It’s a challenge to keep up with
more than one village. Don’t attempt something
like this until you have the hang of it with one.

Starting Village
At the start of a campaign, your village
instutitions include a blacksmith, crypt, general
store, inn, and temple. As a group, choose one
other institution already established in your
village. All these institutions are 1st level. Then
come up with a name for your village as a group.

Village Cycle
Village cycles track when events occur, when
new instutitions get established and upgraded,
and when merchants restock. A village cycle is 10
days long.

Prosperity
Your village’s Prosperity is a numerical
representation of how happy and economically
well off the people of your village are. As
instuitions improve, so do opportunities for the
villagers. The higher the Prosperity, the more
villagers care about each other, and seek to
protect and help their neighbors. If Prosperity
falls, the village’s infrastucture begins to degrade.
With less money coming into the town, there
are fewer opporunities for villagers, and many

become destitute or turn to crime to live.

At the start of a campaign, your village’s
Prosperity is 0, which is average. It’s not doing
amazing, but it’s not doing terrible. This number
is important in determining item availability
(see Trade) and the type of events your village

experiences (see Village Events).

Raising Prosperity

When you invest the money to found or
upgrade an instution, you increase your village’s
Prosperity by 1. The highest Prosperity a village
can have is 10.

Lowering Prosperity

When a village cycle ends without any new
instutituions being founded or upgraded in your
village, its Prosperity decreases by 1. The lowest
Prosperity a village can have is -10.
Village Events

At the end of each village cycle, the Ref rolls for
an event that occurs during the next village cyle
(which happens whether or not the PCs are in
the village). This event impacts the village or
the PCs in some way. The higher the village’s
Prosperity, the more likely the event is to be
a boon to the PCs. The lower the Prosperity,
the more likely the event is to have a negative
impact.
When a village event occurs, roll on the Village
Event table to determine what happens. Unless
otherwise specified, when an institution, item,
or PC is referenced by the table, that instutition,
item, or PC is randomly chosen by the Ref.
[Playtest Note: The Village Event table will have more
events, thus decreasing the odds for any one event.]

Trade
You can buy or trade goods in your village,
provided you have merchants who can supply
them. A merchant’s description in Institutions
lists what the buy and sell.

Buying and Availability

When you want to purchase an item from a
merchant, you must check to see if the item is
available for sale. Find the merchant you wish to

purchase the item from and check its availability,

which is a percentage number found on the

merchant’s table in Instutions. Roll a d100,
and if the result is equal to or lower than the
item’s availability on the table plus the village’s
Prosperity, then it is available. If the item isn’t
available, you can’t attempt to purchase it again
from that merchant until a new village cycle.
If the item is available and you choose not to
purchase it, it is available for purchase for the
rest of the current village cycle, and when a new
cycle starts, you must roll to see if it is available
if you wish to purchase it then. If more than one
merchant could sell the item, you can check for
its availability at each new merchant.
Unless otherwise noted, items are purchased for
the price listed in the rules, either in a booklet or
on their cards.

Selling
Items can be sold in the village to an appropriate
merchant. The money gained from the sale is
a percentage of the item’s base cost based on
the village’s Prosperity, as shown on the Sale
Percentages table.

Sale Percentages
| Prosperity | Sale Percentage |
| --- | --- |
-10                       30%
-9 to -6                    40%
-5 to -2                    45%
-1 to 1                     50%
| 2 to 5 | 55% |
| --- | --- |
| 6 to 9 | 60% |
| 10 | 70% |

Village Crafting
Some institutions, called artisans, provide
crafting services for PCs. If the PC can’t find
a craftable item they want and wish to craft it
themselves, they can pay the instution with a
high enough crafting bonus to do it.

- To begin the project, the institution must be
given the crafting materials for the item and
payment, which is the item’s full price.

- The instiution makes one crafting roll per day

toward crafting the item (because they have

more than just your business on their plate).
The institution’s stats include the bonus you
should add to this crafting role. You should keep
track of the crafting points.

- If you pay twice as much, the institution makes
it a rush job and makes two crafting rolls a day
for the item.
- When the item is complete, you can visit the
instituion and pick it up.

Village Event
| d10 + | Event |
| --- | --- |
Prosperity

institution.

instutitions levels decrease by 1

level before the decrease, one is
destroyed instead.

Institutions

Instutitions are the merchants, temples, and
other sites, services, and organizations that you
can interact with while you are in the town.

You should write down and keep track of each
institution’s level and it’s steward, which is the
person who owns or is otherwise in charge of it.
Stewards are NPCs played by the Ref. When you
found an institution in your town, the Ref can
provide the name and personality of the steward,
or they can ask the player whose character
invests in the institution to create the steward.
Each instutition has the following details:

- Founding Price: The price to establish the
institution in your village. When you pay to
establish an institution, it doesn’t start operating
until the start of the next village cycle.
- Services: The services provided by the
instution are detailed here. Some services are
only availble when an institution achieves a
specific level.
- Level: Each institution has a level that increases
when you pay to advance it. An institution’s
starting level is 1. Its maximum level is detailed
on its advancement table.
- Advancement Table: An instutition’s
advancement table shows the price to increase
an instution’s level, and the benefits that come
from advancing the institution. When you
pay to upgrade an institution, it doesn’t start
operating at its new level until the start of the
next village cycle. Merchant item availability is
shown on this table. When rolling for an item’s
availability, remember to add your village’s
Prosperity to the availability before rolling.

### Alchemist
**Founding Price:** 3,000 gc
Artisan
The alchemist can craft alchemy items that
require a +1 bonus and starting at 2nd level can
craft items that require a +2 bonus.

### Merchant
The alchemist provides the following services:

- Alchemy Items: They buy and sell any items
that require the Alchemy skill to craft. The
availability of items depends on the Alchemy
skill bonus required to craft them.
- Alchemist’s Tools: They buy and sell standard,
fine, and masterwork alchemist’s tools.
- Identify Alchemy: They can provide the
properties of any alchemy item brought to them
for a fee of 10 gc per item
- Alchemy Workshop: For a price of 5 gc a day,
you can rent space in the alchemist’s workshop.
For the day you do this, you have access to their
facilities, which grant you a bonus on alchemy
crafting rolls equal to the alchemist’s level.
- Loyalty Health Potions: When the alchemist
is 5th level and the village’s Prosperity is 10,
healing potions have 100% availability and once
per cycle when you buy something from the
alchemist, they give you a free healing potion.

Advancement Table
The Alchemist Advancement table shows the

availability of alchemy items based on the skill

bonus required to craft them, the bonus the

institution makes to alchemy crafting rolls, and

the price increase the alchemist’s level.

Alchemist Advancement

| Lvl | Price | Avl +1 | Avl +2 | Craft |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1st | - | 40% | 0% | +1 |
| 2nd | 750 gc | 50% | 0% | +2 |

| 4th | 3,000 gc | 70% | 40% | +4 |
| 5th | 6,000 gc | 80% | 60% | +5 |

Auction House
**Founding Price:** 2,000 gc
### Merchant
The auction house has the following services:

- Buy Any Valued Item: They sell any item that
has a price, including gems and art objects. To
figure out the cost of an available item, roll any
die. If the result is even, the item is discounted
by 1d6 × 10%. If the result is odd, the item costs
1d6 × 10% more.
- Buy Monster Parts: You can monster body
part crafting materials at the house. The cost of
one of these items is 2d10 × 100 gc.
- Buy Unique Items: On rare occaision the
auction house sells a unique item, such as a
magic horn of which only one exists in the
world. You can only roll for the availability of
such an item if the Ref allows it. The cost of
such an item is 2d10 × 1,000 gc, or 1d10 × 100 gc
if it can become useless after being used.
- Sell Anything: You can sell any item at the
house. Instead of the normal price, the item
sells for 1d10 × (10 + village Prosperity)% of
its value. If you sell a monster part or a unique
item, use the calculations in the bullets above to
determine the base value before the sale price.
Once you commit to selling an item at auction,
it must be sold, regardless of the sale price
rolled. You can buy it back for whatever it sold
for plus an additional 10% of the item’s value.

- High Roller Auction: When the auction

house is 5th level and the village’s Prosperity is

10, once per cycle when you sell an item at the

auction house and the item would sell for less

than 80% of its sale price, you get 80% of the
sale price instead.

Advancement Table
The Auction House Advancement table shows

the availability of items based on if they are a
valued item, a monster part, or a unique item.

| 3rd | 1,000 gc | 15% | 5% | 0% |
| 4th | 2,000 gc | 20% | 10% | 5% |

### Blacksmith
**Founding Price:** 3,000 gc
Artisan
The blacksmith can craft blacksmithing items

that require a +1 bonus and starting at 2nd level
can craft items that require a +2 bonus.

### Merchant
The blacksmith provides the following services:

- Blacksmithing Items: They buy and sell any
items that require the Blacksmithing skill to
craft, including barding. The availability of
items depends on the Blacksmithing skill bonus
required to craft them. Ammunition for bow
weapons is always available at the blacksmith.
- Crafting Materials: They buy and sells the
items listed on the Crafting Materials for Sale
table in Crafting Materials.
- Smith’s Tools: They buy and sell standard, fine,
and masterwork blacksmith’s tools.
- Enchanted Weapons and Armor: They buy
and sell magic weapons and armor, with base
availability becoming greater than 0 at 4th level.
- Armor Repair: They can repair any armor you
have for 5 gc per suit or shield repaired.
- Blacksmith Workshop: For a price of 5 gc
a day, you can rent space in the blacksmith’s
workshop. For the day you do this, you have
access to their facilities, including the forge,
which grant you a bonus on blacksmithing
crafting rolls equal to the blacksmith’s level.

- Hone Weapon: When the blacksmith is 5th
level and the village’s Prosperity is 10, they can
hone a non-unarmed weapon for 500 gc. The
weapon gains a +1 bonus to damage which
doesn’t stack with additional honing from any

blacksmith. If you get a doom on an attack with
this weapon, it is no longer honed.

Crypt

**Founding Price:** 2,000 gc
Honor the Fallen
When a crow dies during an adventure, their
remains (whatever that may be), can be brought
to the crypt and interred there. When the crow
is interred, their player picks a boon from the list
below. Once per cyrcle, the dead crow can instill
upon another crow who prays at their grave
gains the boon. A crow can only benefit from one
boon at a time, and only one crow can have that
dead crow’s boon at a time. If you already have a
boon and pray for another, the new one replaces
the old one.
Expending a boon requires no action. After you
expend the boon, you no longer have it.

- Boon of Cooperation: When you make an
assist, you can expend this boon to grant an
additional bonus to the test you aid equal to
twice the crypt’s level.
- Boon of Disappearance: You can use this boon
to become invisible for a number of combat
rounds equal to the crypt’s level.
- Boon of Escape: You can expend this boon

to teleport a number of squares equal to three
times the crypt’s level.
- Boon of Flight: You can use this boon to gain a
flying speed equal to your speed for a number of
combat rounds equal to the crypt’s level.
- Boon of Fury: When you deal damage with
an attack, you can expend this boon to deal
additional damage to the target. You deal an
additional d6 damage to the target for each level
the crypt has.
- Boon of Greed: You can expend this boon
when you are in a dungeon to learn the
direction of the most valuable treasures on
the same level as the dungeon as you and how
many chambers, including hallways, you must
pass through to reach them. The number of
treasures you learn about is equal to the crypt’s
level, in order from most valuable to least.
- Boon of Knowledge: When you expend this
boon, choose a specific creature, place, event,
or organization with a proper name that you
know. You can ask the Ref up to a number of
questions equal to the crypt’s level about the
subject, which they must answer honestly.

- Boon of Rescue: After you roll a RR, you can
use this boon to improve the result by 1 tier.
You can use this boon a number of times equal
to the crypt’s level before it is expended.
- Boon of Swiftness: When you expend this
boon, your speed increases by a number equal to
the crypt’s level until the end of the DT.
- Boon of Vitality: When you regain Stamina,
you can expend this boon to regain additional
Stamina equal to twice the crypt’s level.

When the crypt is 5th level and the village’s
Prosperity is 10, it is considered 6th level for
boon effects.

Inn
**Founding Price:** 1,000 gc
### Merchant
You have a home in your village, so you don’t
need to stay the night at the inn. However, if
you’re in another town (or want to stay at the
inn in your village for some reason), the cost of a
night at the inn, which includes a day’s worth of
food, is 5 gc.

Gambling

As a rest activity, you can gamble at the common

room of the inn. The maximum bet you can place
is based on the inn’s level. Minimum bet is 1 gc.
You can play the following games at the inn.

- Over/Under 7: State whether you think
the result of a 2d6 roll will be over, under, or
exactly 7 and make a bet. Then roll the dice. If
you were wrong, you lose your bet. If you were
right and guessed over or under 7, your money

is doubled. If you guessed a result of exactly 7
right, your money is quadrupled.
- Guess the Number: You must guess a number
from 1 to 6. Then the Ref rolls a d6. If you
guessed the result incorrectly, you lose your bet.
If you guessed correctly, your winnings are five
times your bet.
Find It Here
When the inn is 5th level and the village’s
Prosperity is 10, you can find a special traveling
merchant at the inn. Once per cycle, you can
purchase any item that has a price from this
merchant, but the cost of the item is increased
by 25%.

level and the village’s Prosperity is 10, it is

Wheelwright

[Playtest Note: Information about the wheelwright

Your Home
Every player character has a small living space
they call home within their village. This house
is a secure place to keep equipment and treasure
that you keep stocked with food. While you’re
in your village, you don’t need to worry about
tracking money to spend on food and shelter.

## Retirement
When your PC has 50,000 or more TXP, you
can retire them to the village. If you do, your
PC becomes an NPC and no longer delves into
dungeons. You can give your equipment to a
worthy successor, perhaps a new PC you create
or one of the other crows your retired adventurer
delved alongside. A retired PC gives up the
dangerous life of dungeon delving.
When you retire to the town, choose one of the
following benefits that the town gains. If you
have 100,000 or more TXP, you can choose two
benefits:
[Playtest Note: More retirement benefits will be in a
future version of the test.]

- Best Steward Ever: Your retired PC becomes
the steward of an institution in the village
(taking over from the previous steward). This
institution always operates as if the village’s
Prosperity is 10, even if it is lower.
- Crow Daddy: Your retired PC is sure to
give helpful tips to new crows. Choose one
starting trait your retired PC knows. When
you or another player creates a new PC from
the village, that PC can start with that trait in
addition to any other traits they start with. A
new PC with 0 TXP can start with a maximum
of three traits.
- Generous Benefactor: Your retired NPC still
loves to hear stories about the crow life and
hangs out at the inn common room or a tavern
listening to all the latest adventures. Once per
cycle, the PCs can visit your retired PC who
gives them a gift rolled on the [Playtest Note:
insert table name here ... coming soon].
- Master Mentor: Choose up to three trait your
retired PC knows. Other PCs can learn these
traits from your retired PC, making them cost
half as much XP as they normally do.
- Work With My Hands: Your retired PC goes
to work at an artisan institution in the village.
This institution gains an additional +5 bonus to
crafting rolls.

# Part 3: Monsters & Animals



This book contains creature stats meant for the ref to use, though players may access the animal stats for use with spells like _animal form_ . 

[ _Playtest Note: The current list of monsters includes just what is needed for the playtest: some animals, some blood creatures, and one unique creature. More creatures of all types will be in future tests._ ] 

### **Monsters** 

The term “monster” is used in this game to refer to creatures who are not humans or animals. There are six monster types in the core game: 

- **Angels:** Though called angels for their otherworldly home and beauty, these creatures are far from heavenly. They radiate magic that charms and warps the minds of their prey. 

- **Blood Creatures:** Almalgamations of flesh and fluid of humans and animals, blood creatures are held together by the same magic that makes them ravenous for the flesh of other creatures. Most are animalistic in nature. 

- **Demons:** Beasts summoned from the pits of Hell, demons slaughter other creatures for fun. They’re fast, strong, and tenacious. 

- **Plants:** Animated by necromancy, these plants use viney tendrils and roots to move, stab, and choke prey to quench a literal bloodthirst. 

- **Undead:** The reanimated remains of humans, twisted into killing machines that abhor anything alive. 

- **Uniques:** Some monsters in this book are unique experiments of the necromancers. Most have a level of sapience the other monsters lack and there exists only one of each such creature in Cornath. 

### **Monster Names** 

Except for uniques, each monster doesn’t have a specific name. Instead, a monster is given a letter after it’s type for referencing in the rules, like “blood creature A” or “undead C.” The arrival of monsters threw the world into chaos, so there is no universal nomenclature for them. 

Each monster has art for you to show the players, so they can come up with names for them. There’s a list of coloquial names that NPCs might use to refer to a monster so the Ref doesn’t have to come up with them on the fly (but they still can). 

### **Creature Stats** 

Most creatures have the same stats as PCs with the following exceptions. 

### **Slots** 

Monsters don’t have slots and die when they are reduced to 0 Stamina. Humans and pet animals have the potential to be allies, so they do have slots, which counts as backpack slots for them. 

If a PC or other creature with backack slots gains the stats of a different creature, they keep their original number of backpack slots. 

### **Power** 

A creature’s power signifies their general toughness and threat to other creatures. In this booklet, a creature’s power is scaled from 0 to 50, though future products could go even higher! The greater the power, the more deadly headto-head combat is with the creature. The more experienced the PCs are, the more likely they are to survive an encounter with a creature of high power. Starting PCs stand no chance in a headto-head battle against creatures of power 11 or greater (and even creatures of power 1 or more in groups can really mess them up). 

### **Darkness No Penalty** 

Monsters have special senses that mean darkness and dim light impose no penalty on them. If a human or an animal has a similar trait, it is called out in their stat block. 

### **X/Rest** 

So creature’s have a feature that has “X/Rest” written next to it. The creature can only use this feature X number of times between rests. 

### Playtest Material 

This is playtest material that has yet to be edited or properly laid out. Give us your feedback on the game in our playtest survey here: https:// forms.gle/VyEqwYKGiiZ5QBxc6 

### **Animals** 

Animals can be friend and foe alike to crows. Some of the following creatures are available as pets (see _Characters_ ). Others are wild predators who might see the PCs as their next meal. 

Animals available as pets have slots. If a PC turns into an animal through the use of magic, add the slot from the animal stat block to their Stamina total while in the animal form instead.

### **Blood Creatures** 

Craelin created blood creatures—amalgamations of fluid and flesh of several creatures brought together in the form of an entirely new predator. Blood creatures have exposed musculature and red features. They know on some instinctual level that their existence is unnatural, and this knowledge drives them to feast on the flesh of humans and animals as a way to prove they are superior and belong in the world. 

### **Blood Creature A** 

**Common names:** creep, red drool, spindleclaw 

These blood creatures hunt in packs, dropping from above onto prey. They drool bright red saliva when food is near. 

|**Blood Creature A**|**Blood Creature A**|||

|**Agility:**1|**Mind:**-2|**Strength:**0||
|**Attack**|**Range**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|**Claws (+1)**|Melee 1|2 dam|3 dam|
|**Drop Attack**||||



This creature treats falls as 10 squares shorter when calculating fall damage. After falling and taking no damage, they gain a +1 bonus on attacks and damage until the end of the turn. 

### **Blood Creature B** 

**Colloquial names:** blood spider, mouth eyes 

An orb of tendrils split by a toothy maw that parts to reveal glowing red eyes, this blood creature clings to surfaces and their enemies.

### ***Tendril Snare** 

When this creature gets a tier 3 result on a tendril attack against a Medium or smaller target, the target is grabbed. While the target is grabbed this way, this creature’s bite attack deals an addtional 2 dam to them, and this creature can use a maneuver to deal 2 damage to the target. 

### **Blood Creature C** 

**Colloquial names:** blood brute, tiny arms 

A malleable creature that most often resides in somewhat humanoid form, this monster has the power to knock down doors to get at prey but prefers to flow through them.

### **Ring Collector** 

The Ring Collector is a creature gifted with supernatural strength and speed who is obsessed with finding and collecting magic rings. They invaded and wiped out several villages during the Necromancer War in pursuit of rings.. 

The Ring Collector is Namlin, a farmer who lost his family to a fire during the war. He knew of a legendary ring that could bring people back from the dead and went about finding as many magic rings as he could. Each warped him. One even erased his memory, but not his obsession to find magic rings, and so now he has no purpose beyond the hunt. 

|**Ring Collector**|**Ring Collector**|||

### ***Literally Kneel** 

When this creature gets a tier 3 result with a Kneel attack, the target falls prone. 

### ****Exploding Sword** 

When this creature’s sword attack kills a creature, the target explodes and deals 1d6 dam to each creature within 1 square. 

### **Extra Action** 

This creature can take one additional action or maneuver on their combat turn. 

### **Vanish (1/Rest)** 

This creature can teleport up to 50 miles to a location they have visited before regardless of line of effect. 

### **Ring Sense** 

This creature knows the exact location of any magic ring within 20 squares of them regardless of line of effect.

# Part 4: Adventure - The Blood Library



Before Craelin the Clever became Craelin the Craven, he built a respository of books to share his knowledge of alteration magic with the world. As he fell into wickedness, the necromancer covered this library in flesh and filled it with blood creatures to protect what he once shared. With Craelin gone, ambitious crows have hacked through the protective scab to form an entrance into the Blood Library, where books and other treasures are ripe for picking. 

The PCs know where the Blood Library is and thanks to Karina, a lone crow who recently lost the rest of her group right outside the place, they know there’s still treasure inside. 

### Guidelines Not Handcuffs 

The text here presents the challenges, but can’t account for every way to overcome them. Don’t let that get in the way of fun. If the PCs have a clever idea that isn’t accounted for in the text, let them try it! If it makes sense and should just work, it does. If it seems risky, call for a test. 

### **Travel** 

Use the Overland Travel section in _The Rules_ to run travel to the library. The Blood Library is 36 miles through normal terrain of light forest from the PCs’ village. The PCs know the way and have passed by outside of the library before its flesh was hacked open. The area the PCs journey through has a base EN of 6. The season is fall. 

### **Describing the Journey** 

As the PCs travel to the library, they notice the following environmental details: 

- The green leaves of forest trees have changed to a blood red color as autumn is in full swing. 

- Small game trails carve through the forest growth in the direction of the library. 

- Occaisioanlly large swaths of dried blood appear on the trail, and indication that blood creatures stalk the area. 

- The closer they get to the library the heavier the coppery scent of blood gets in the air and the fewer sounds of birds and other animals. 

### **POI: Tower Ruin** 

When the PCs are 18 miles into their journey to or from the library, they find: 

- A ruined towered, completely collapsed into a pile of stone rubble and rotting timbers. 

- A ring of stones around a hole in the ground that used to be a well. 

If the PCs investigate, roll an encounter check. 

### **Collapsed Tower** 

A PC who investigates the rubble finds some of the stones are marked with arcane runes, a sign that this place once belonged to a mage. One or more PCs can search through the densely-packed rubble, doing so takes 4 hours of travel time, cutting the PCs base travel speed in half and earning them another encounter check. 

If the PCs wish to search faster as to waste no travel time, they can use brute force. If they go this route, each PC searching makes this test. 

### **2d10 + S + Endurance skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|Take 1d6+3 P<br>dam|Take 1d6 P<br>dam|No effect|



Each PC who searches the rubble gets a treasure from the Tower Treasures table. The tower can produce six treasures and then no more. 

### **Tower Treasures** 

|**d6**|**Treasure**|
|---|---|
|1|2d10gc|
|2|Moonstonegem (worth 35gc)|
|3|Spinelgem (worth 75gc)|
|4|_Summon object_spellbook*|
|5|Fine enchanter’s tools*|
|6|_Boom wand_*|



*Item can only be found once. Reroll subsequent restuls. 

### Playtest Material 

This is playtest material that has yet to be edited or properly laid out. Give us your feedback on the game in our playtest survey here: https:// forms.gle/VyEqwYKGiiZ5QBxc6 

### **Well** 

The well descends into darkness and has smooth sides leading down 40 feet to a dusty dry bottom where a human skeleton in tattered rags sits slumped on the ground with a shovel in hand. 

A PC who climbs down into the well notices the skeleton’s left is broken. The remains belong to a crow who broke their leg climbing into the well and buried treasure in the hopes of returning for it if they were rescued. They weren’t. 

If a PC digs into the earth of the well, they find a coin purse that contains 234 gc. 

### **Travel Encounters** 

Use the Travel Encounters table when an encounter occurs during the journey. Blood creatures and predatory animals attempt to kill and devour the PCs. Blood creatures fight to the death. Animals flee when their Stamina falls below half their maximum. 

### Blood Creatures 

Each blood creature has no official name, though their stat blocks present a list of possible colloquial nicknames the people of Cornath might have for them. To make the world feel more authentic, the rules provide art for each creature you can show to the players and have them come up with the name they wish to give a creature after encountering them. 

### **Travel Encounters** 

|**d6**|**Treasure**|
|---|---|
|1|2d6**blood creature A**|
|2|1d6**blood creature B**|
|3|1**blood creature C**|
|4|2d6**wolves**|
|5|1**bear**|
|6|Traveling alchemist (see below)|



### **Traveling Alchemist** 

The heroes stumble upon **Micah** , an eyepatchwearing, traveling alchemist in his sixties who is trying to get to the PCs’ village. He’s terrified of bandits on the road, but also lost. He approaches the PCs cautiously, asking where they’re from. If they’re friendly, he asks for directions. 

If the PCs tell Micah the way back to the village, he gives them a _healing potion_ as thanks and asks if they wish to buy any of his alchemy items, which he is willing to sell to them at a 50% 

discount. If the PCs threaten Micah, he acts as if he hurls his fire bomb at them and tries to flee. If he survives 1 round of combat and can’t escape, he surrenders his equipment to them and pleads for his life. 

Micah’s supplies are low, but he carries the following items: 

- 3 vials of blood creature blood (Tiny crafting materials) 

- 5 vials of acid 

- 5 vials of poison 

- 2 _healing potions_ 

- 1 fire bomb 

- 1 set of alchemist’s tools 

- 2 rations 

- 1 bedroll 

This encounter should occur only once. If it is rolled again, reroll for a new encounter. 

||**Micah**<br>**Size:**Medium|**Power:**4|**Type:**|Human|
|---|---|---|---|---|
||**Stamina:**10|**Speed:**5|**Slots:**|10|
||**Agility:**1|**Mind:**1|**Strength:**-1||
||**Attack**|**Range**|**12-16**|**17+**|
||**Unarmed (+1)**|Melee 1|2 dam|3 dam|
||**Acid Vial (+1)**|Ranged 5|5 dam|9 dam|



### **Fire Bomb** 

Micah hurls a bomb up to 10 squares. It explodes in a 3 cube. Each creature in the cube must make an Agility RR. 

||**≤11**||**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|---|---|
||10|dam|5 dam|0 dam|



### Awarding Treasures 

If the PCs find a magic item they’ve never encountered before, they aren’t away of the item’s properties (though they can tell the item is magic based on how it vibrates when touched). You can give the players a generic card when they find a magic item instead of the specific item’s card to keep properties hidden. If the players find a _boom wand_ , give them the magic wand card. 

### **Library Approach** 

When the PCs reach the Blood Library, they notice the following details: 

- An enormous hunk of scabby flesh covered in giant, moving eyes weeps blood and pus into a red lake. 

- The lake flows into a tall, wide wound in the scab that leads to stone steps that rise up out of the red. 

The blood lake and libraries entrance are depicted on the Blood Library map. 

### **Blood Lake** 

A PC can make the following test to see what they know about the blood lake. 

### **2d10 + M + Monster Lore skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|No<br>info|Blood creatures<br>are attracted to<br>blood|A creature covered in<br>blood can’t hide from<br>a blood creature|



The blood lake is 3 feet deep and easy to wade through, though a PC who enters the lake has their clothes stained with blood. A PC can clean the blood from their clothes as a rest activty. 

The PCs may be able to avoid wading through the blood by climbing the fleshy mound that surrounds the library. No test is required if they use sharp weapons or tools to stab into it to hold their balance. They may also cut down trees to build a bridge to cross. 

### **Library Interior** 

**When the PCs enter the library, the first dungeon turn begins.** Let them know, and start the 30-minute timer. 

### **Shared Details** 

- **Floor Heights:** The second floor of the library is 15 feet above the first. The ceiling of the second floor is 15 feet high. 

- **Shelves:** A creature who can reach an empty bookshelf can use an action to attempt to knock it over on a creature adjacent to the shelf. 

### **2d10 + S + Lift skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|No<br>effect|Pusher and target<br>take 1d6 dam and<br>are knocked prone|Target takes<br>1d6 dam and is<br>knocked prone|



- **Map:** Areas of the library are keyed to the Blood Library map. 

- **Noticeable Details:** Every area starts with a bulleted list of details the PCs notice when they enter for the first time, provided they can discern the details in the darkness. 

### **Library Encounters** 

Unless otherwise noted, blood creatures in the library fight the PCs to the death. A PC who is covered in blood automatically fails tests made to sneak and hide from blood creatures. 

The EN for the library is 6. If at least one PC is bloodstained by the lake, the EN is 5. If half or more of the PCs are bloodstained, the EN is 4. When a random encounter occurs, roll on the Library Encounters table. 

### **Library Encounters** 

|**d6**|**Treasure**|
|---|---|
|1|1d6**blood creature A**|
|2|2d6**blood creature A**|
|3|1**blood creature B**|
|4|1d6**blood creature B**|
|5|1**blood creature C**|
|6|2**blood creature C**|



Unless otherwise noted, interior areas of the library share the following details: 

- **Darkness:** The interior areas of the library have no light sources. 

- **Flesh-Covered Walls:** The walls of the library are covered in scabby flesh and can be climbed without a test by PCs who stab into the walls with sharp weapons or tools. 

### **1. Entrance** 

This area is filled with dim light thanks to the open entrance leading outdoors. 

- A massive stone desk stands before three sets of doors. 

- A pearl-handled letter opener is wedged through the handles of a south-facing cabinet double door at the bottom of the desk. 

### **Cabinet** 

The letter-opener is a Tiny art object worth 50 gc. If it is removed from the cabinet door, the **blood creature B** trapped inside of it bursts forth, eager to devour the closest PC. 

### **2. Library Ground** 

- Mostly empty shelves hold a few ruined books. 

- Spiral staircases in each corner lead up to a balcony that lines the walls above. 

- ( _If the blood creatures are still area 3_ ) The sound of several mouths gnawing on something wet and juicy comes from above. 

### **Blood Creatures** 

The blood creatures in area 3 come to investigate if the PCs make any loud noises or if at least one of them is bloodstained and spends more than a minute in this area (perhaps searching shelves). 

### **Shelves** 

The shelves here are mostly empty save for a lore book (Magic Lore) that can be found if a PC spends 5 minutes searching the shelves. 

### **3. Library Balcony** 

- Mostly empty shelves line the back wall of a balcony that overlooks more library below. 

- A closed cabinet stands on the west wall, just south of a door, painted with a red droplet. 

- ( _If the blood creatures haven’t gone to area 2_ ) A dozen blood creatures, six-armed, short humanoids with razor teeth, cling to the walls here, feasting on the flesh. 

### **Blood Creatures** 

The twelve **blood creature A** immediately attack any PC they notice. If the PCs flee this area, the creatures don’t pursue, content to continue munching on the walls. 

### **Shelves** 

The shelves here are mostly empty save for two lore books (Historical Lore and Nature Lore) that can be found if a PC spends 5 minutes searching the shelves. 

### **Locked Cabinet** 

The cabinet is locked and trapped. A PC who examines the keyhole notices a small arcane rune carved above it and can decipher its meaning with the following test. 

### **2d10 + M + Magic Lore skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|No<br>info|The rune<br>means<br>“fire.”|The rune is a magic trap.<br>If the lock is tampered,<br>the cabinet explodes.|



If the key in area 6 is used to unlock the cabinet, the trap is disarmed. A PC can attempt to pick its lock, taking a -1 penalty on the attempt if they use a tool other than a lockpick set. 

### **2d10 + A + Pick Lock skill** 

|**2d10 + A +**|**Pick Lock skill**||
|---|---|---|
|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|The trap<br>triggers.|The cabinet is<br>open with enough<br>time time for the<br>opener to grab one<br>item from it before<br>the traptriggers.|The cabinet<br>unlocks and<br>the trap is<br>disarmed.|



If a creature attempts to force the cabinet open with brute Strength or damages the door or lock, the trap triggers. When the trap triggers, the cabinet explodes and everything inside of it is destroyed. Each creature within 2 squares of it must make an Agility RR. 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|2d6 dam|1d6 dam|0 dam|



The cabinet contains a _healing potion_ , a _shrink_ spellbook, a poison vial, and gold ink pot, a Tiny art object worth 100 gc. 

### **4. Prison Crystal** 

This area is one 30-foot-high chamber that is filled with dim light emenating from the huge crystal at the center of the chamber. 

- A soft-glowing purple crystal with a 10-foot diameter and nearly 30-foot height rises out of the floor toward the vaulted ceiling. 

- The silhouette of a  cloaked figure with ten arms holding a large sword floats at the center of the crystal. 

- A spiral staircase next to the crystal rises 15 feet into the air to meet a catwalk that circles the crystal and leads to an iron throne that touches the glowing stone. 

The heavy crystal is built into the floor and has 50 Stamina. A creature who touches the crystal or sits in the throne can communicate telepathically with the entity trapped inside. If a crow wants to recall lore about the entity, they can make the following test. 

### **2d10 + M + Monster Lore skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|No info|They know<br>the info in the<br>first paragraph<br>ofThe Ring<br>Collector.|They know the<br>info in the first<br>two paragraphs<br>ofThe Ring<br>Collector.|



### **The Ring Collector** 

The Ring Collector is a creature gifted with supernatural strength and speed who is obsessed with finding and collecting magic rings. They invaded and wiped out several villages during the Necromancer War in pursuit of rings. 

The Ring Collector is Namlin, a farmer who lost his family to a fire during the war. He knew of a legendary ring that could bring people back from the dead and went about finding as many magic rings as he could. Each warped him. One even erased his memory, but not his obsession to find magic rings, and so now he has no purpose beyond the hunt. 

When a PC touches the crystal, they recieve a telepathic message from Namlin, asking calmly and softly for freedom and promising rewards. The PC can respond telepathically and have a conversation with Namlin as long as they remain touching the crystal. If asked about their background, Namlin claims to have been captured by Craelin and refers to themself as “the Collector” if asked for a name. 

Namlin knows much of the library’s secrets and is willing to give the following information to the PCs in exchange for freedom. They give one of the following details the PCs don’t already know to prove their knowledge, but provide no other information about the library until they’re free: 

- How the trap on the locked cabinet works in area 3 and the location of its key in area 6 

- The alcove hidden behind the shelf in area 5 

- How the trap in area 7 works and which book to select to prevent it from happening 

- The properties of a magic item found by the PCs in the library 

If the PCs ask about the sword, Namlin promises to give it to them if they are freed and bring them the _minor telekinesis ring_ from area 8. 

If Namlin is freed and the PCs don’t have the ring, the collector shares any promised secrets and then leaves the chamber, walking directly to area 8, breaking down the door, and moving inside to grab the ring. All blood creatures cower in Namlin’s presence, staying at least 5 squares away and only attacking creatures who attack them first or are more than 5 squares from the collector. The collector takes the _minor telekinesis ring_ from area 8 then vanishes from the library. 

If the PCs give Namlin the ring after freeing them, the collector gives them an magic greatsword which has the exploding property (see _Characters_ ). If the PCs have the ring and don’t give it to Namlin, Namlin fights until the ring is surrendered. Whenever Namlin gets the ring, they vanish from the library. 

Namlin came to the library in search of a ring that belonged to Craelin, and the necromancer captured him. Craelin stripped the Ring Collector of their collection and imprisoned them in this crystal before disappearing. 

### **5. Laboratory** 

- A large stone table carved with glyphs holds the corpse of a wolf-sized spider, thirteen rotting, dead crabs, a bucket of blood, and a journal. 

- Empty jars sit on a shelf on the north wall. 

- A spiral staircase leads up. 

### **Blood Creature Ritual** 

The journal has extensive notes on a magic ritual to create a blood creature. A PC who studies the journal as a rest activity can attempt to perform the ritual. The ritual requires the special table in this area, the corpses of a giant spider and thirteen crabs and bucket filled with blood. Attempting the ritual consumes the materials used in it. The PC performing the ritual must make the following test. 

### **2d10 + M + Necromancy skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|The ritual<br>materials are<br>destroyed and<br>the PC takes 3d6<br>P dam. If this<br>kills the PC, they<br>become a blood<br>creature C under<br>the Ref’s control.|The PC creates a<br>blood creature B<br>that they know<br>will remain<br>under their<br>control until the<br>end of the DT or<br>harmed by the<br>PC or their allies.|The PC<br>creates<br>a blood<br>creature<br>B that is<br>now a<br>pet they<br>own.|



### **Shelves** 

A PC who searches the shelves finds the _blood concoction_ , a unique blood-red potion. 

A PC who examines the room for secrets can make the following test. If a PC searches the floor in front of the shelves specifically, they automatically get a tier 3 result on the test. 

### **6. Private Quarters** 

- Rotted timbers and torn cloth litter the floor of this chamber, sitting atop a shallow, wide puddle of blood. 

- A small crack in the west wall is just big enough for a person to squeeze into. 

- A spiral staircase leads down. 

### **Difficult Terrain** 

The floor of this chamber is difficult terrain. 

### **Blood Puddle** 

A PC suspicious of the puddle can make this test. 

### **2d10 + M + Monster Lore skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|No<br>info|The PC knows the<br>blood is related to<br>blood creatures but<br>unsure how.|The PC knows<br>the puddle is a<br>sleeping blood<br>creature C.|



If a creature enters the room without sneaking or touches the puddle, then the puddle, which is an unconscious **blood creature C** , awakens. A creature trying not to wake the blood creature must make the following test to sneak into and out of the room and if they interact with the coins or iron bars in the alcove. 

### **2d10 + A + Sneak skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|The<br>creature<br>wakes.|The creature remains<br>asleep but stirs. If any<br>PC makes this test and<br>gets this result again,<br>the creature wakes.|The<br>creature<br>reamins<br>asleep.|



### **Alcove Treasure** 

### **2d10 + M + Search skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|No<br>info|The PC notices<br>scratches on the<br>floor in front of<br>the shelf.|As tier 2, but the<br>PC also infers this<br>means the shelf<br>is often moved to<br>get at something<br>behind it.|



If the shelf is moved, the PCs find an alcove behind it that contains 3 vials of blood creature blood (Tiny crafting materials) and pile of 89 gc. 

The alcove contains a 2-foot-long wood chest that is locked. It can be easily smashed or forced open. Doing so wakes the blood creature. A PC can attempt to pick the lock with this test. 

### **2d10 + A + Pick Lock skill** 

|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|---|---|---|
|The chest<br>stays locked.<br>The creature<br>wakes.|The chest<br>unlocks, but the<br>noise wakes the<br>creature.|The chest<br>is unlocked.<br>The creature<br>stays asleep.|

The chest contains a _stubborn object_ spellbook, 5 iron bars (Medium crafting materials), 229 gc, and a hand-written note on a piece of parchment that says “Gold clasps.” The chest also contains a red key with a head shaped like a blood droplet. 

### **7. Forbidden Books** 

The door to this chamber from area 2 is locked, and can be unlocked with the key in area 6. A creature can attempt to pick the lock or break the door down with the following test: 

### **2d10 + A or S** 

|**2d10 + A or S**|||
|---|---|---|
|**≤11**|**12-16**|**17+**|
|Door remains<br>locked, and the<br>attempt makes<br>prolonged noise.|The door opens,<br>and the attempt<br>makes prolonged<br>noise.|The<br>door<br>opens.|



The door has 50 Stamina. Attacking the door makes prolonged noise. Any prolonged noise draws the attenion of the blood creatures in area 3 and also triggers an encounter check. 

When the PCs enter the area, share these details: 

- A spiral staircase at the center of the room leads from the floor up to a metal trapdoor. 

- The flesh-coated walls in this room are covered in green, throbbing pustules. 

- The room’s five longest walls each have a leather-bound tome chained at the center. 

- The books are identical, save for the metal of their clasps, which are, starting on the westmost wall and going clockwise: gold, silver, copper, brass, and platinum. 

- A metal drain grate sits on beneath the stairs. 

- The bleached bones of humanoids litter the floor. 

### **Acid Pustules** 

If a creature touches a book other than the one with gold clasps or gets violent with a pustule, a trap triggers. Begin tracking the game in combat rounds. At the start of the first round, the door and trapdoor in this room shut and lock (if not destroyed), and neither can be unlocked with the key from area 6 while the trap is active. The trapdoor has the same statistics as the door in this chamber that leads to area 2. 

Also at the start of the first round, the pustules pop and begin spraying constant streams of a flesh-eating acid into the room.  At the end of the first round, the acid in the room reaches a height of 5 feet. The height is 10 feet by the end of the second round, and the entire room is flooded with acid by the end of the third round, which is also when the acid stops streaming. 

If the door to area 2 is opened, the acid instead flows out into that area, creating a puddle of acid that is 1-foot deep and moves 5 squares west at the end of each round. All acid drains at the of the DT. 

If a creature starts their turn in or enters the acid, they take 1d6 damage. If they are fully submerged in the acid, they take 2d6 damage instead. 

### **Books** 

The gold-clasped book is a _deadspeech_ spellbook. The other books are each empty journals. 

### **8. Museum** 

The door to this area from area 3 is locked. It has the same statistics as the locked door described at the start of area 7. 

- Four black stone pedestals each hold up a different treasure. The southmost holds two books, the southwest holds a chest, the northwest holds a ring, and the northmost holds a mask made of jade. 

- An open metal trapdoor in the center of the floor gives way to a descending spiral stair. 

- Six blood creatures shaped like spiders walk on the ceiling of this chamber. 

### **Blood Creatures** 

There are six **blood creature B** on the ceiling. They move to attack PCs who enter the room or make prolonged noise opening the door. 

### **Treasures** 

The books on the pedestal area fine lore book (Monster Lore) and a _corrupt_ spellbook. The ring is a _minor telekinesis ring_ , the jade mask is a Tiny art object (300 gc), and the chest contains 423 gc.

# Part 5: Appendices & Reference Sheets

# Appendix A: Player Cheat Sheet

## MCDM_Crows_Cheat_Sheet_for_May_June_2026_Playtest

```
CROWS Cheatsheet for May June 2026 Playtest
```

### Tests 

- 2d10 + Characteristic Score (+ Skill) 

- Bonuses and penalties can apply per Ref 

- Natural 2 or 3 is a doom 

- 11 or less failure, bad, or least good 

- 12-16 is partial progress or mixed result 

- 17+ is good 

- Natural 19-20 is a crit 

### Damage 

- Reduces Stamina (AD first if wearing armor) 

- When Stamina is 0 you start taking wounds 

- Wounds fill backpack slots 

- When all backpack slots full of wounds = dead 

### Dungeon Turn (DT) 

- Each is 30 minutes or real time. 

- Roll DT usage dice at the end. 

- Encounter check at the end. 

### Resting 

- 6 hours long, must spend 4 sleeping 

- Encounter check every 2 hours 

- Must eat ration and have bed(roll) 

- Regain all Stamina 

- Remove 1 wound 

- Can perform 1 rest activity 

### Usage Dice 

- Always d6s 

- Roll when item is used or end of DT 

- If 1 or 2 comes up, remove that usage die 

- All usage dice gone? Item/effect stops 

- Spellbooks regain all usage dice on rest 

### Counter 

● If missed with a melee attack, you can counter 

### Inventory 

- 2 hand slots, 2 belt slots, and 10 backpack slots. 

- Like items (daggers, wands) can be stacked. 

- Hand items are equipped. 

- Armor takes up backpack slots when worn. 

- Belt items can be drawn as a maneuver. 

- Backpack items drawn as a maneuver. Roll 1d10. If result meets or beats the slot, you draw the item. If lower, nothing drawn, can rearrange. 

### Combat Actions and Maneuvers 

- 1 action, 1 maneuver, 1 reaction 

- **Common Maneuver:** Move speed, knockback, grab, escape grab, shift, draw/pick up item, stand up 

- **Triggered:** Opportunity attack/counter 

- **Crit on Attack:** Extra action 

- **Flanking:** +1 on melee attack roll 

_**Grab: 2d10 + S + Unarmed skill**_ 

- _≤11:_ The target can counter. 

- _12-16:_ The target is grabbed by you and can 

- counter you. 

- _17+:_ The target is grabbed by you. 

_**Escape Grab: 2d10 + A or S + Unarmed skill**_ 

- _≤11:_ The grabber can counter. 

- _12-16:_ You are no longer grabbed, and the grabber can counter. 

- _17+:_ You are no longer grabbed.. 

_**Knockback: 2d10 + S + Unarmed skill**_ (Melee 1) 

- _≤11:_ The target can counter. 

● _12-16:_ push 1 

● _17+:_ push 2 

### Conditions 

- **Grabbed:** Speed 0, +1 on attacks against you 

- **Prone:** -1 on melee attacks, +1 on melee attacks against you, -1 ranged against you, speed halved 

- **Boned:** -1 on all tests and +1 damage dealt to you on incoming damage equal to boned level. Disappears at DT end. 

- **Blessed:** +1 on all tests and +1 damage you deal equal to blessed level. Disappears at DT end. 

- **Boned and Blessed:** Cancel out effects 1 to 1. 

### Doom 

- Doom on casting spell = Backlash 

- Doom on melee = 17+ counter dam is 17+ result 

- Doom on ranged = 17+ dam dealt to adjacent ally 

### Ranged into Melee 

- Attack a target adjacent to ally and miss, hit ally instead for 12-16 result. 

- Attack adjacent target with ranged attack, -1. 

### Critical Hit 

- Critical hit with attack gives extra action 

- Critical hit with spell no usage die roll for book 

### Armor 

If you wear armor, it gives you AD. When you take damage, it comes from AD first. When AD hits 0, your armor is broken. AD can be restored by repairing armor as a rest activity. 

### Chaos Count 

When a PC gets a 11 or lower result that isn’t doom while casting a spell, the Ref adds 1d6 + the spell’s rank to the chaos count. When the Ref has 13 chaos, the triggering spell has a backlash. 

- Counter is 12-16 result of melee weapon in hand 

### Light 

- **Dim Light:** Hard to see. -2 penalty on tests. 

- **Darkness:** Can’t see. -4 penalty on tests.
